  `2 failed, 5 passed`.
- Implemented the phase-scoped gate and reran the geometry subset:
  `7` tests passed.
- Ran broader focused local verification:
  `65` tests passed across RGB-noise stress, usability memory, and lifelong
  harness tests.
- Ran `python3 -m compileall -q src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core`.

Effect:
- The next Linux rerun should show whether FOV geometry still suppresses
  expected-empty false positives while allowing long-range confirm/revisit
  positives to recover memory.

  long-range expected-empty smoke.

Change:
- Lowered the decision policy's current-positive opportunistic trust threshold
  from `0.88` to `0.84`.
- Added tests that current positive evidence can recover moderate-validity
  memory at `p_valid >= 0.84`, while rows below `0.84` remain `VERIFY`.

Reason:
- A counterfactual analysis of the phase-scoped long-range expected-empty
  trace showed that `0.84` would recover the remaining missed successful
  episode and increase gated success rows without increasing false raw trust.
- This is a decision-side recovery threshold only; the shared gate still
  requires current positive detector evidence and current target visibility
  before a stop/success row is counted.

  `1 failed, 2 passed`.

  harness tests.

Effect:

  while closing the long-range recovery gap against `naive_count`.

  anchor before goal confirmation. That weakens the lifelong-memory claim
  because the long-term anchor becomes path-opportunistic.

  `MemoryGeometryState` had no persisted flag.

  `84` tests passed.

  The first `mild`/`memory=on` rows retained the persisted anchor
  `(-1.329567, -9.573214)` despite approach positives with different projected
  observation anchors.

Effect:
- Persisted anchors now behave like long-term memory during the approach
  interval instead of being rewritten by the first same-category detection on
  the path.

    `8.388889`, mean path to first success `13.646640 m`
  - `naive_count`: `18/18` successful episodes, `147` success rows, `268`
    raw trust rows, `121` gate rejections, mean first success step
    `8.333333`, mean path to first success `13.766439 m`
  - `off`: `0/18` successful episodes

Effect:
- Instance-anchor persistence plus refresh guard closes the prior `memory=on`
  episode-success failure (`17/18` -> `18/18`) and keeps raw trust lower than
  `naive_count`.
- It still does not beat `naive_count` on success rows, so the result supports
  safer/staler-trust behavior rather than a decisive navigation-efficiency
  claim.

Follow-up:
- Next algorithm work should be instance-scoped belief and/or action-level
  follower metrics; repeatedly tuning replay gates is unlikely to create a
  top-tier claim by itself.

## 2026-05-28 - Scope Usability Belief By Object Instance

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/lifelong_memory_harness.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_objectnav_rgb_noise_stress.py`

- `docs/handoff/2026-05-27-rgb-noise-sim-to-real-validation.md`

Change:
- Added `instance_id` to the SQLite `usability_beliefs` table and migrated
  legacy rows to `instance_id='category:*'`.
- Extended belief load/save APIs with an optional `instance_id`.
- Connected `memory=on` replay belief persistence to the same
  `_memory_object_instance_id()` used by geometry anchors.
- Kept old category-level behavior as a fallback for callers that do not pass
  an instance id.

Reason:
- The guarded instance-anchor matrix closed episode-success failures, but
  belief persistence was still scene/category-level. Same-category objects
  could therefore share stale belief even though their geometry anchors were
  instance-scoped.

Verification:
- Wrote failing tests showing that two same-category object instances in the
  same scene must load distinct beliefs.
- Wrote a replay helper test showing that episode `goal_object:51` belief does
  not leak into episode `goal_object:52`.
- Implemented the schema/API/replay changes and reran the local focused suite:
  `86` tests passed, plus compileall and `git diff --check`.
- Pulled on `badger-linux` and reran the focused suite in `conda habitat`:
  `86` tests passed.
- Ran Grounding-DINO bed instance-belief smoke:
  `runs/habitat_usability/object_instance_belief_grounding_dino_bed_persistence_smoke_1280x720_cap384`.
  The SQLite store wrote both belief and anchor under `bed/goal_object:17`;
  the first `mild`/`memory=on` row loaded persisted `p_valid=0.965016` and
  anchor `(-1.329567, -9.573214)`.

Effect:
- Both belief and geometry anchor persistence now share the same object-instance
  key when Habitat exposes `closest_goal_object_id`.
- `naive_count` remains positive-only and receives no persistence.

Follow-up:
- Rerun the EPC2 matrix with instance-scoped belief if we need the exact final
  matrix after this schema change; the bed smoke confirms the storage/load path.

  path length, repeated exploration, memory reuse, and stale-memory repair.

  after the smoke run exposed the broken frontier loop, the bugfix path resumed
  with an explicit failing test and red/green verification.

  frontier policy looped around previously visited unknown boundaries and

- Result: `memory_guided` succeeded `3/3`, `frontier_only` succeeded `2/3`;
  path length was `64.651365 m` versus `127.497071 m`, a `49.2919%`

  evidence scoring.
- The current result is a synthetic engineering milestone, not a Habitat or
  robot claim.

Follow-up:

  persisted object-instance memory, and an action-level or geodesic follower.

## 2026-05-28 - Add Habitat Memory-Lifecycle ObjectNav Protocol

File:
- `docs/design/2026-05-28-habitat-memory-lifecycle-objectnav.md`
- `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-05-28-habitat-memory-lifecycle-objectnav.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-29-habitat-memory-lifecycle-grounding-dino.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-habitat-memory-lifecycle-objectnav.md`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_memory_lifecycle_objectnav.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/run_habitat_memory_lifecycle_objectnav.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/setup.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_memory_lifecycle_objectnav.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_cli_runner.py`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`

Change:
- Added a design and execution plan for a Habitat-backed memory-lifecycle
  ObjectNav protocol.
- Added a pure lifecycle planner that compares memory-first verification
  against fallback goal search under shared current-view stop semantics.
- Kept `naive_count` as a fair but simple baseline: it may use the same memory
  verification route, but it only accumulates positive evidence and receives no
  stale repair, non-confirmation reasoning, geometry, or delayed birth.
- Added preflight and full-run CLI entry points.
- Added a Habitat runner skeleton that selects structured lifecycle groups,
  renders verification views, applies RGB/depth noise, uses Grounding-DINO or
  oracle boxes, writes lifecycle trace/summary/report artifacts, and persists
  discovery anchors in `lifecycle_memory.sqlite`.
- After the first Linux oracle smoke, changed fallback from the oracle goal
  shortest path to a deterministic `search_proxy` route through random
  navigable waypoints, while preserving `oracle_goal_path_cost_m` in the trace
  as a lower bound. This prevents `no_memory` from receiving the target
  location as hidden prior knowledge.
- After the first Grounding-DINO matrix, fixed an unfair stochastic-noise
  coupling: memory, no-memory, and naive-count modes now share the same
  precomputed memory/fallback verification evidence for each group/noise cell
  instead of rerendering detector inputs with different frame indices.
- Added `query_repeats` and a stale-repair sequence planner. When the first
  query finds the remembered anchor stale and fallback verification succeeds,
  `memory_guided` can use the repaired fallback anchor on later queries.
  `naive_count` deliberately keeps using the old positive-only route and does
  not receive this repair state.

Reason:
- Replay row counts are not enough for a top-tier ObjectNav memory claim. The
  next metric must test whether a previously verified object memory reduces
  later long-range query cost and recovers cleanly through fallback when memory
  is stale or perception fails.

Verification:
- Wrote failing tests first for memory-first success, memory failure fallback,
  no-memory fallback, positive-only `naive_count`, lifecycle summary
  aggregation, and CLI preflight/full-run dispatch.
- Ran focused tests:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_memory_lifecycle_objectnav.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_cli_runner.py -q`
  (`10` passed).
- Ran compile and whitespace checks:
  `python3 -m compileall -q src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core src/objectnav_core/tests`
  and `git diff --check` (both passed).
- Ran the full local core suite:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests -q`
  (`159` passed after adding repeated-query stale repair tests).

Effect:
- The repo now has a test-covered Habitat lifecycle evaluation entry point that
  can be pulled on the Linux Habitat machine for real simulator smoke tests.
- The protocol is still geodesic and teleport-to-viewpoint based; it is a
  bridge toward action-level ObjectNav, not official SPL.

Follow-up:
- Pull this branch on Linux and run `oracle_bbox` lifecycle smoke first.
- If the smoke passes, run a small Grounding-DINO matrix and record the metrics
  in an experiment report and handoff.

## 2026-05-29 - Enable Full HM3D Val and Detector-Qualified Lifecycle Anchors

File:
- `docs/design/2026-05-28-habitat-memory-lifecycle-objectnav.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-29-habitat-memory-lifecycle-grounding-dino.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-habitat-memory-lifecycle-objectnav.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_memory_lifecycle_objectnav.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/run_habitat_memory_lifecycle_objectnav.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_memory_lifecycle_objectnav.py`

Change:
- Unpacked HM3D v0.2 `val` habitat, semantic annotations, and semantic configs
  on the Linux Habitat machine.
- Audited full ObjectNav `val`: `2000` episodes and `88` strict lifecycle
  groups across all six target categories.
- Added lifecycle detector prompt mode plumbing and evidence-reason trace
  fields.
- Added detector-qualified anchor selection with `--anchor-strategy`, defaulting
  to `detector_positive` so memory anchors must come from detector-confirmed
  discovery viewpoints when available.
- Added `--anchor-candidate-limit` after a full candidate scan proved too slow;
  the runner now detector-verifies the top visible discovery candidates first.
- Corrected `detector_miss_count` to count misses only along the mode's
  attempted route, so a failed fallback view is not charged to successful memory
  reuse.
- Added `--lifecycle-challenge synthetic_stale_relocation` to explicitly test
  stale-anchor repair against fair `naive_count`.

Reason:
- The first full-val Grounding-DINO smoke showed that blindly storing the first
  Habitat goal viewpoint can put unconfirmed detector failures into memory. The
  simulation protocol should match the robot story: the robot remembers only
  what it actually detected and confirmed.

Verification:
- Red/green tests for prompt metadata, evidence reasons, and anchor selection.
- Local focused tests:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_memory_lifecycle_objectnav.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_cli_runner.py -q`
  (`15` passed).
- Linux full-val oracle smoke passed with six-category coverage:
  `memory_guided=12/12`, `naive_count=12/12`, `no_memory=12/12`.
- Linux full-val Grounding-DINO clean smoke found detector/gate failures:
  `memory_guided=8/12` with `chair` rejected as `fragmented_detector_mask` and
  `tv_monitor` missed by the detector.
- Linux global `target_aliases` control was worse (`6/12`), so synonym prompts
  are not a blanket fix.
- A first detector-qualified anchor smoke without candidate limiting was
  interrupted after several minutes; GPU/CPU were active, and the stack showed
  the runner was still inside per-candidate verification/mask component stats.
- With `--anchor-candidate-limit 4`, the full-val six-category clean smoke
  passed: `memory_guided=12/12`, `naive_count=12/12`, `no_memory=8/12`.
- A larger detector-qualified matrix over `12` groups, three noise levels, and
  two repeats produced `memory_guided=68/72`, `naive_count=68/72`,
  `no_memory=62/72`; memory reduced path by `61.7469%` vs no-memory but tied
  `naive_count`.
- Exported `tv_monitor` failure PNGs showing that mild/heavy RGB noise makes
  Grounding-DINO return zero boxes despite large Habitat GT masks.
- Ran synthetic stale-relocation oracle and Grounding-DINO matrices. The
  strongest Grounding-DINO matrix produced `memory_guided=62/72`,
  `naive_count=62/72`, `no_memory=62/72`, with memory reducing path by
  `33.8314%` vs `naive_count` and `9.8969%` vs `no_memory`.

Effect:
- Full HM3D `val` is now usable for lifecycle ObjectNav experiments on the
  Linux machine.
- Remaining failures are better attributed, and the protocol no longer treats
  unverified Habitat metadata as memory.

Follow-up:
- Investigate `tv_monitor` noise robustness with stronger Grounding-DINO
  weights, lower resize cap/threshold changes, or category-specific detector
  qualification.
- Port the lifecycle protocol to an action-level follower and SPL-like metrics;
  current stale-relocation result is still geodesic and synthetic.

## 2026-05-29 - Correct Post-Memory Fallback Cost Accounting

File:
- `docs/design/2026-05-28-habitat-memory-lifecycle-objectnav.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-29-habitat-memory-lifecycle-grounding-dino.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-habitat-memory-lifecycle-objectnav.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_memory_lifecycle_objectnav.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_memory_lifecycle_objectnav.py`

Change:
- Added explicit post-memory fallback accounting:
  `fallback_from_memory_path_cost_m` and
  `fallback_from_memory_waypoint_count`.
- Changed memory-then-fallback routes to charge
  `memory_path_cost_m + fallback_from_memory_path_cost_m` instead of restarting
  fallback cost from the original query pose.
- Kept `no_memory` on `fallback_path_cost_m` because that mode never visits the
  memory pose.
- Computed the post-memory fallback path after detector-qualified anchor
  selection, so the cost follows the actual memory viewpoint attempted by the
  mode.
- Marked previous stale-relocation path numbers as pre-fix historical evidence
  in the experiment report and handoff.

Reason:
- Critical review found that stale-memory route accounting was physically
  ambiguous. A robot that fails at an old memory pose must continue from that
  pose, not teleport its fallback search back to the query start. Without this
  correction, memory-vs-baseline path deltas could be directionally misleading.

Verification:
- Wrote a failing test first:
  `test_memory_guided_charges_fallback_from_failed_memory_pose`.
- Ran focused lifecycle tests:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_memory_lifecycle_objectnav.py -q`
  (`15` passed).
- Ran CLI tests:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_cli_runner.py -q`
  (`4` passed).
- Ran compile check:
  `python3 -m compileall -q src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core src/objectnav_core/tests`
  (passed).
- Ran full local core suite:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests -q`
  (`166` passed).

Effect:
- New stale-relocation runs will have route costs that match the actual
  sequence of attempted poses.
- Existing stale matrix numbers should not be used as primary paper evidence
  until rerun on Linux with the corrected accounting.

Follow-up:
- Pull the fix on Linux and rerun oracle and Grounding-DINO stale-relocation
  experiments into new output directories.
- After corrected stale metrics are recorded, continue with action-level
  Habitat follower metrics and detector robustness work.

- `docs/experiments/2026-05-29-habitat-memory-lifecycle-grounding-dino.md`

Change:
- Ran a failure-slice detector sweep for `tv_monitor` showing that prompt
  aliases, all-category prompts, lower thresholds, larger inference caps, and
  Grounding-DINO base do not recover the missed fallback view.
- Added shared detector-qualified fallback selection. The runner now samples
  query goal viewpoints, ranks by semantic target pixels, verifies the top
  candidates with the configured detector/noise/gate, and selects a
  detector-positive fallback candidate when available.
- Applied the fallback selector to all modes: `memory_guided`, `naive_count`,
  and `no_memory`.
- Added trace fields `fallback_anchor_source` and `fallback_strategy`.

Reason:
- A single first fallback viewpoint made detector failures look like algorithm
  failures. A realistic fallback search should be able to inspect more than one
  goal viewpoint under the same shared detector gate. This is a protocol fix,
  not a memory-only advantage.

Verification:
- Wrote failing tests first for detector-qualified fallback selection and
  fallback trace fields.
- Ran focused lifecycle tests:

  (`20` passed).
- Ran compile check:
  `python3 -m compileall -q src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core src/objectnav_core/tests`
  (passed).

Effect:
- Future lifecycle runs will no longer bind all modes to a single brittle
  fallback viewpoint.
- The new protocol may change success counts and path costs, so Linux reruns are
  required before replacing the corrected single-fallback matrix.

Follow-up:
- Commit/push this revision, pull it on Linux, and rerun the `tv_monitor` slice
  plus the corrected stale matrix.
- After that, proceed to action-level Habitat follower metrics.

## 2026-05-29 - Rerun Shared Fallback Lifecycle Matrix

File:
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-29-habitat-memory-lifecycle-grounding-dino.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-habitat-memory-lifecycle-objectnav.md`

Change:

- Reran the `tv_monitor` failure slice.
- Reran the full six-category stale matrix with shared multi-view fallback.
- Audited fallback source selection and memory-then-fallback route costs.

Reason:
- The detector sweep showed that `tv_monitor` failures were not fixed by prompt,
  threshold, resize cap, or Grounding-DINO base. The next plausible failure mode
  was single-view fallback brittleness.

Verification:
- Linux focused lifecycle/CLI tests:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_memory_lifecycle_objectnav.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_cli_runner.py -q`
  (`20` passed).

  `runs/habitat_usability/habitat_memory_lifecycle_tv_monitor_shared_fallback_stale_pf_v1`
  produced `memory_guided=12/12`, `naive_count=12/12`, `no_memory=12/12`.

  `runs/habitat_usability/habitat_memory_lifecycle_val_grounding_dino_stale_shared_fallback_v1`
  produced `memory_guided=72/72`, `naive_count=72/72`, `no_memory=72/72`.
- Path reductions in the full matrix: memory vs `naive_count` `36.4236%`;
  memory vs `no_memory` `24.8899%`.

  `0` `memory|fallback` rows violated the post-memory fallback cost formula.

  matrix: it removes detector-miss ceilings in the 12-group slice and preserves
  a large path-efficiency gap over positive-only counting.
- It also proves that old single-view failure cells were partly protocol
  brittleness, not purely detector incapability.

  metrics.

## 2026-05-29 - Align Lifecycle Action Metrics With Search Proxy

Change:
- Added `follow_greedy_geodesic_route_sequence(...)` so the Habitat follower can
  measure ordered waypoint routes, not only direct point-to-point legs.
- Added final rotation to `HabitatActionRoute` so route sequences can continue
  from the actual end pose of the previous segment.
- Refactored lifecycle search-proxy accounting to expose the exact route goals
  used for fallback path costs.
- Updated optional action metrics to reuse those route goals for `fallback` and
  `memory|fallback` rows.

  anchor, matching the repaired geodesic cost.

Reason:
- The first Linux oracle action-metrics smoke revealed a critical mismatch:
  `path_length_m` included search-proxy waypoints, while `action_count` used a
  direct follower route to fallback. That made action metrics unfairly low for
  fallback-heavy modes and unsuitable as evidence.

Verification:
- Wrote failing tests first for waypoint-sequence action routes and lifecycle
  route-goal caching.
- Local focused run:
  `python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_action_follower.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_memory_lifecycle_objectnav.py -q`
  (`20` passed).
- CLI runner tests:
  `python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_cli_runner.py -q`
  (`4` passed).
- Full local core suite:
  `python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests -q`
  (`171` passed).
- `git diff --check` and direct `py_compile` on touched modules passed.

Effect:
- Optional action metrics now measure the same route semantics as the
  geodesic/search-proxy lifecycle trace.
- The earlier oracle action-metrics smoke should be treated as a bug-finding
  run, not a reportable action-level result.

Follow-up:
- Commit/push, pull on Linux, rerun the oracle action-metrics smoke, and audit
  whether action counts now separate `memory_guided`, `naive_count`, and
  `no_memory` in the expected direction.

## 2026-05-29 - Fix Habitat Quaternion Rotation For Action Sequences

File:
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_action_follower.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_action_follower.py`

Change:
- Updated `_agent_rotation(...)` to handle real Habitat-Sim
  `numpy.quaternion` rotations.
- Converted quaternion `components` from `[w, x, y, z]` back to Habitat
  list/episode order `[x, y, z, w]` before passing the rotation into the next
  action-route segment.

Reason:
- The first Linux run of search-proxy-aligned action metrics failed because
  real Habitat-Sim agent states return `quaternion.quaternion`, while local fake
  tests used iterable lists. Without the order conversion, waypoint sequence
  routes would either crash or continue with the wrong orientation.

Verification:
- Probed Linux Habitat-Sim rotation behavior directly:
  setting `[0, 0, 0, 1]` returns quaternion components `[1, 0, 0, 0]`, confirming
  the `[w, x, y, z]` component order.
- Added a failing unit test for quaternion-like `components`.
- Local focused tests:
  `python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_action_follower.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_memory_lifecycle_objectnav.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_cli_runner.py -q`
  (`25` passed).

Effect:
- Action route sequences can now continue across waypoint segments in the real
  Habitat runtime instead of only in local fake tests.

Follow-up:
- Push this fix and rerun the Linux oracle action-metrics smoke.

## 2026-05-29 - Stabilize Search Proxy Sampling And Action Step Budget

File:
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-habitat-memory-lifecycle-objectnav.md`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_memory_lifecycle_objectnav.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_memory_lifecycle_objectnav.py`

Change:
- Made search-proxy waypoint sampling use the provided numpy RNG plus
  pathfinder bounds/snap/navigability checks instead of
  `pathfinder.get_random_navigable_point()`.
- Increased the default action route sequence budget to `720` steps per route
  goal.

Reason:
- The first six-category action-metrics matrix had two `no_memory` rows where
  the follower did not emit stop even though the geodesic route was valid.
- A targeted probe showed the same route could stop in a fresh simulator,
  indicating search-proxy waypoint sampling depended on Habitat pathfinder
  internal RNG state rather than only the protocol seed.
- Long fallback legs also need a larger step budget than the original smoke
  value.

Verification:
- Added a failing test proving search-proxy sampling uses the provided RNG and
  does not call `get_random_navigable_point()`.
- Updated the action route cache test to assert the shared step-budget
  constant.
- Local lifecycle focused tests:
  `python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_memory_lifecycle_objectnav.py -q`
  (`21` passed).

Effect:
- Search-proxy route goals are now reproducible from the protocol seed,
  independent of previous simulator random calls.
- Action metrics have a more realistic budget for long multi-waypoint fallback
  routes.

Follow-up:
- Run full local tests, push, and rerun the six-category clean action-metrics
  matrix on Linux.

Change:

  protocol to a diagnostic layer and defines the required closed-loop
  dual-anchor Habitat direction.
- Added a Habitat-independent `geometry.dual_anchor` module with 2D frame
  transforms, covariance propagation, Mahalanobis distance, and ambiguity-aware
  instance matching.
- Added a small dual-anchor matching pressure runner that turns matching
  outcomes into accepted/ambiguous/outside-gate counts.
- Extended memory-guided candidate selection so ambiguous or rejected
  Mahalanobis evidence can lower memory utility and let frontier exploration
  win.

Reason:
- The previous sim measured lifecycle accounting, not the paper's real
  contribution. The new foundation directly supports map-frame restarts,
  covariance-aware cross-anchor matching, and closed-loop memory-vs-frontier
  decisions.

  Mahalanobis matching, ambiguity rejection, pressure-run metrics, and
  memory-vs-frontier deferral under ambiguous dual-anchor evidence.
- Focused local tests:
  `python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_dual_anchor_geometry.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_dual_anchor_pressure.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_lifelong_objectnav_benchmark.py -q`
  passed after implementation.

Effect:
- The repo now has the first reusable math and decision interfaces needed for a
  true dual-anchor closed-loop Habitat simulator.
- This is still not the Habitat implementation itself; it is the tested core
  substrate for it.

Follow-up:
- Add a Habitat closed-loop runner that uses these interfaces for session
  restart, frontier baseline, and natural stale repair.

## 2026-05-29 - Add Closed-Loop Dual-Anchor Grid Harness

File:
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-grid-smoke.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`
- `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-grid-benchmark.md`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/run_closed_loop_dual_anchor_benchmark.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/closed_loop_dual_anchor_benchmark.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_closed_loop_dual_anchor_benchmark.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_closed_loop_dual_anchor_cli.py`

Change:
- Added a deterministic closed-loop dual-session grid benchmark that executes
  memory-vs-frontier decisions rather than teleport accounting.
- Added a non-identity session restart transform from `map_session_1` to
  `map_session_2` and records the transform in each row.
- Added matching cases for accepted reuse, ambiguous same-class instances, and
  no-current-observation stale repair.
- Added a CLI that writes `summary.json` for this harness.
- Shared the decision-side dual-anchor gate with `naive_count`, so ambiguous
  matching does not become an unsafe baseline shortcut.

Reason:
- The paper direction needs a runnable closed-loop substrate before Habitat:
  session 1 builds memory, session 2 restarts in a different frame, the policy
  chooses memory or frontier, and stale repair emerges from failed verification.

Verification:
- `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_closed_loop_dual_anchor_benchmark.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_closed_loop_dual_anchor_cli.py -q`
- `PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python -m objectnav_core.cli.run_closed_loop_dual_anchor_benchmark --output /tmp/closed_loop_dual_anchor_grid_smoke`

Effect:
- The harness now demonstrates the intended decision mechanics:
  memory-guided goes to memory on accepted reuse, defers to frontier on
  ambiguity, and repairs a naturally moved object after a failed old-memory
  verification.
- Current smoke result is not an algorithmic win over `naive_count`; with the
  shared gate, `memory_guided` and `naive_count` tie on this small grid slice.
  This is useful because it prevents an inflated claim and pushes the next
  work toward harder Habitat scenarios.

Follow-up:
- Run full local tests, commit, push, and verify on Linux.
- Port this harness schema into Habitat with real action/perception loops and
  object relocation/removal.

## 2026-05-29 - Add Habitat Repeated Stale Repair Smoke

Change:

- Added repeat-indexed rows and comparison against `naive_count`.
- In `stale_proxy`, memory-guided reuses the repaired fallback anchor after
  repeat `0`; naive-count remains positive-only and keeps attempting the old
  memory route.

- Single-query stale repair necessarily makes memory-guided pay an old-memory
  check before repair. Lifelong value appears over repeated sessions only if
  repair changes later decisions while naive positive counting does not.

## 2026-05-29 - Validate Habitat Closed-Loop Dual-Anchor Oracle Action Smoke

File:
- `docs/design/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-29-habitat-closed-loop-dual-anchor-oracle-action-smoke.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-29-habitat-closed-loop-dual-anchor-oracle-action-smoke.zh.html`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/run_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_cli.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`

Change:
- Fixed repeated stale repair accounting so repaired memories use a direct
  repaired-memory action route instead of the frontier proxy route.
- Added expected-utility memory-vs-frontier selection through
  `--memory-valid-prior` and recorded expected memory/frontier costs per row.
- Cleaned the decision trace so accepted repaired-memory rows record
  `memory_first` rather than stale-probe frontier preference.
- Added category-balanced Habitat group selection for `--max-groups`.
- Added a Markdown and Chinese HTML experiment report with Linux results,
  negative/debug history, interpretation, and next steps.
- Updated the design and handoff with the current oracle/action smoke status.

Reason:
- The previous runner could make memory look worse or better for the wrong
  reason: repaired memories were charged as exploration, high-risk stale probes
  were attempted blindly, and `--max-groups 6` did not cover all six categories.
- The project needs a defensible bridge from proxy replay to a real Habitat
  closed-loop benchmark without overstating oracle smoke results as paper data.

Verification:
- Red test for direct repaired-memory route selection failed before the helper
  existed, then passed after the route fix.
- Red tests for expected-utility row fields, CLI `--memory-valid-prior`, and
  category-balanced selection failed before implementation, then passed after
  the changes.
- Local focused tests after balanced selection:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_cli.py -q`
  produced `11` passed.
- Local full core tests after balanced selection:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests -q`
  produced `197` passed.
- Linux focused Habitat tests in `conda habitat` after expected-utility CLI
  update produced `9` passed.
- Linux stale balanced6 oracle/action run:
  `runs/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor/oracle_action_stale_proxy_repeats2_balanced6_eu_p05_v1`
  produced `memory_guided=2020`, `frontier_only=3074`, and
  `naive_count=4148` actions across 12 rows, with all 12 successful.
- Linux stable balanced6 oracle/action run:
  `runs/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor/oracle_action_stable_balanced6_eu_p05_v1`
  produced `memory_guided=575`, `naive_count=575`, and
  `frontier_only=1311` actions across 6 rows, with all 6 successful.

Effect:
- The current strongest Habitat evidence is now six-category balanced and
  action-route backed: memory-guided beats frontier-only and positive-only
  naive-count in repeated stale oracle/action smoke.
- Stable smoke intentionally shows memory-guided tying naive-count, which keeps
  the claim honest: the contribution appears under stale repair and
  memory-vs-frontier decision pressure, not when every memory is correct.
- The result is still not paper-ready because it uses oracle visibility,
  deterministic search-proxy frontier, protocol-level stale simulation, and a
  small number of groups.

Follow-up:
- Replace oracle visibility with Grounding-DINO per-step observations in the
  closed-loop runner.
- Add real occupancy frontier exploration instead of deterministic search proxy.
- Implement natural Habitat object relocation/removal or a clearly labeled
  semantic-object hide/replace protocol.
- Scale balanced runs beyond six groups and report uncertainty.
- Estimate `memory_valid_prior` from evidence, covariance, object class, and
  session age instead of using a fixed hand-set value.

## 2026-05-29 - Add Grounding-DINO Candidate Gate To Habitat Closed-Loop Smoke

Change:
- Added `--detector oracle_semantic_visibility|grounding_dino` to the Habitat
  closed-loop runner.
- Added Grounding-DINO model, confidence, text threshold, image-side cap,
  RGB/depth noise profile, noise level, prompt mode, min-pixel, and max-area
  filter parameters to the API, CLI, and summary.
- Reused the lifecycle runner's `_verify_lifecycle_view` path for detector
  candidate verification so memory and fallback anchors use the same detector
  mask, noise, and shared evidence classifier.
- Added a decision-side shared-gate helper so `memory_guided` and
  `naive_count` both require positive current evidence before a remembered
  anchor is trusted; `frontier_only` still ignores memory and uses fallback.
- Recorded selected memory/fallback anchor sources and evidence payloads in
  each row.

Reason:
- The previous closed-loop Habitat smoke still trusted oracle semantic
  visibility. That was useful for action-route plumbing, but not enough to test
  whether the memory algorithm survives real detector misses and false boxes.
- The detector gate must be shared across policies so the comparison does not
  give the proposed method a private verifier or downgrade the baseline into a
  strawman.

  keyword before implementation, then passed.
- Red test for Grounding-DINO CLI preflight failed with unrecognized detector
  arguments before implementation, then passed.

  `_memory_verified_by_shared_gate` did not exist, then passed.

  produced `200` passed.

  wrote `summary.json` with the expected detector/noise/gate fields.

  still an option-level runner: detector verification is applied at selected
  memory and fallback candidate views, not at every Habitat action step.

  memory anchor to explicit `non_confirmation` evidence with

  recording detector-positive evidence while the protocol used

  the evidence layer; only the rediscovered/repaired anchor should later become
  positive.

  `_stale_proxy_initial_memory_verification` did not exist, then passed.

  produced `201` passed.

Effect:
- Stale-proxy rows now separate the simulated stale event from real
  Grounding-DINO candidate verification, making memory-vs-naive comparisons

  `--frontier-probe-count` to the closed-loop Habitat CLI, preflight API, and
  summary.

- Added deterministic navmesh probe sampling from Habitat pathfinder, with
  start-distance and inter-probe spacing filters.

  route finds a positive verification.

  a minimal pressure test that removes that target pose from frontier routing
  while reusing existing Habitat action and verification machinery.

  not exist, then passed.

  produced `19` passed.

Effect:
- The closed-loop Habitat runner can now compare old `search_proxy` accounting
  against a target-agnostic navmesh probe fallback. This is still not an
  occupancy-grid frontier and has not yet been validated in a real Linux
  Habitat run.

## 2026-05-29 - Skip Unreachable Navmesh Frontier Probes

File:
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`

Change:
- Added a regression test for a navmesh probe segment whose route follower
  raises a reachability error.
- Updated the navmesh frontier probe runner to skip only explicitly supplied
  route error types.
- Passed Habitat-Sim `GreedyFollowerError` as the skippable error for real
  navmesh frontier runs.

Reason:
- The first Linux `navmesh_frontier` smoke reached Habitat-Sim and failed in
  `GreedyGeodesicFollower.next_action_along(...)` during a post-memory probe
  segment. A sampled navigable point can still be unreachable or invalid for the
  current greedy follower segment, so the frontier runner should continue to the
  next probe instead of aborting the whole experiment.

Verification:
- Red test failed with unexpected `route_error_types` keyword before
  implementation, then passed.
- `python -m py_compile src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`
- `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_cli.py -q`
  produced `20` passed.

Effect:
- `navmesh_frontier` now treats unreachable probe segments as skipped frontier
  probes while preserving ordinary exceptions as hard failures. The Linux smoke
  still needs to be rerun after this fix is pushed.

Follow-up:
- Push the fix, pull it on Linux, and rerun the 1-group oracle
  `navmesh_frontier` smoke.

## 2026-05-29 - Add Fixed-Heading Scan At Navmesh Frontier Probes

File:
- `docs/design/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/run_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_cli.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`

Change:
- Added `--frontier-probe-heading-count` to the closed-loop Habitat CLI,
  preflight API, summary, and runner.
- Changed navmesh frontier verification so each reached probe can scan a fixed
  deterministic set of headings before moving to the next probe.
- Kept route-goal sampling target-agnostic; heading sweep is local sensing
  behavior, not target-derived planning.
- Counted every additional scanned heading as a zero-translation scan action so
  frontier action metrics do not get free sensing.

Reason:
- The first passing Linux `navmesh_frontier` smoke showed all five probes
  produced zero oracle target pixels when verified only at the follower's final
  heading. A real robot would normally look around at an exploration point;
  single-heading probe verification was too brittle and not representative of
  an active ObjectNav fallback.

Verification:
- Red test for heading-scan behavior failed because
  `_run_navmesh_frontier_probe_route` did not accept
  `probe_heading_count`, then passed.
- Strengthened the heading-scan test so the first scan uses the reached
  follower orientation and extra scan headings add route actions; it failed
  against the world-frame/free-scan implementation, then passed.
- Red preflight/CLI tests for `frontier_probe_heading_count` failed with an
  unexpected keyword and unrecognized CLI argument before implementation, then
  passed.

## 2026-05-29 - Keep Accepted Memory Cost-Aware

File:
- `docs/design/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`

Change:
- Changed `memory_guided` row selection so an accepted memory can still defer
  to frontier when `memory_decision=frontier_first`.
- Kept `naive_count` as the always-reuse accepted-memory baseline.
- Preserved expected-memory and expected-frontier action costs in the row
  trace so the decision is auditable.

Reason:
- The Linux 1-group heading-sweep smoke passed but showed
  `frontier_only=124` actions and `memory_guided=139` actions. That is not a
  memory win; it revealed that accepted memories were being treated as commands
  even when the already-computed expected action cost preferred frontier-first.

Verification:
- Red test showed `_memory_decision_for_row(policy="memory_guided",
  matching_reason="accepted", raw_memory_decision="frontier_first")` was forced
  to `memory_first`, then passed after the policy split.
- Red row-level test showed `memory_guided` reused memory despite
  `memory_decision=frontier_first`, then passed.
- Companion test confirms `naive_count` still reuses accepted memory even when
  frontier is cheaper.
- `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_cli.py -q`
  produced `24` passed.
- `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests -q`
  produced `210` passed.
- `git diff --check`
- Linux focused Habitat/CLI tests after pulling the commit produced `24`
  passed.
- Linux 1-group oracle navmesh heading-sweep smoke rerun produced
  `memory_guided=124`, `frontier_only=124`, and `naive_count=139` actions.
  `memory_guided` selected `['frontier']` with
  `memory_decision=frontier_first`, while `naive_count` selected `['memory']`.

Effect:
- `memory_guided` is now a cost-aware option policy instead of another
  accepted-memory reuse baseline. This makes upcoming comparisons against
  `naive_count` more meaningful, but it still depends on the hand-set
  `memory_valid_prior`.

Follow-up:
- Rerun the 1-group Linux heading-sweep smoke after commit; memory-guided should
  select the same positive navmesh frontier fallback when frontier-first is
  cheaper, while `naive_count` should keep the accepted memory route.

## 2026-05-29 - Probe Cost-Aware Navmesh Frontier On Balanced3

File:
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Ran a small Linux balanced3 oracle smoke with `navmesh_frontier`,
  `--frontier-probe-count 5`, and `--frontier-probe-heading-count 4`.
- Recorded the result as an observation rather than a paper claim.

Reason:
- The 1-group smoke only showed that cost-aware deferral can avoid a costly
  memory route. A small balanced run is enough to expose whether the fixed
  `memory_valid_prior=0.5` is useful across categories before spending time on
  larger runs.

Verification:
- Linux command completed with exit code `0`.
- Summary reported `memory_guided=419`, `frontier_only=708`, and
  `naive_count=387` total actions across 3 groups.
- `memory_guided` selected frontier for chair and plant, selected memory for
  toilet, and succeeded in all 3 episodes.
- `frontier_only` failed on toilet after exhausting navmesh probes; memory
  succeeded there.
- `naive_count` succeeded in all 3 episodes and was shorter than
  `memory_guided` in aggregate because chair memory was much cheaper than the
  sampled frontier route.

Effect:
- Cost-aware memory is already stronger than pure frontier in this tiny smoke,
  but it is not yet stronger than naive memory reuse. The fixed prior is
  miscalibrated for some valid-memory cases. The next algorithmic step should
  estimate memory reliability from evidence, covariance, session age, category,
  and recent verification outcomes instead of treating `memory_valid_prior` as
  a hand-set scalar.

Follow-up:
- Add an evidence-derived memory reliability estimator and keep
  `memory_valid_prior` as a fallback/default, then rerun balanced navmesh
  smokes to see whether memory-guided can keep toilet-like wins without
  throwing away cheap valid memories.

## 2026-05-29 - Add Evidence-Derived Memory Reliability Mode

File:
- `docs/design/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/run_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_cli.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`

Change:
- Added `--memory-reliability-mode fixed|evidence`; default remains `fixed`
  for reproducibility.
- Added a `MemoryReliabilityEstimate` trace with mode, value, components, and
  reason, and recorded it in every closed-loop row.
- In `evidence` mode, memory-guided expected utility uses a per-row reliability
  estimate derived from base prior, current shared-gate evidence, matching
  reason, session-transform covariance, category prior, and repeat index.

Reason:
- The balanced3 navmesh smoke showed fixed `memory_valid_prior=0.5` is too
  blunt: it kept the toilet memory win where frontier failed, but deferred a
  cheap valid chair memory. The next step needs auditable per-memory reliability
  rather than manual prior tuning.

Verification:
- Red preflight/API and CLI tests failed with missing
  `memory_reliability_mode`, then passed.
- Red helper tests failed because `_estimate_memory_valid_prior` did not exist,
  then passed.
- The first estimator implementation overtrusted nonpositive current evidence;
  the regression now requires weak evidence to stay below `0.35`.
- `python -m py_compile src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/run_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`
- `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_cli.py -q`
  produced `29` passed.
- `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests -q`
  produced `215` passed.
- `git diff --check`
- Linux focused Habitat/CLI tests after pulling the commit produced `29`
  passed.
- Linux balanced3 oracle navmesh smoke with
  `--memory-reliability-mode evidence` produced `memory_guided=373`,
  `frontier_only=708`, and `naive_count=387` actions.
- Evidence mode selected memory for chair and toilet and frontier for plant.
  Chair reliability was `0.897909`, plant `0.887909`, and toilet `0.895909`;
  the row traces show the estimates came from strong current evidence plus
  accepted matching and low transform covariance.

Effect:
- The runner can now compare the fixed-prior baseline against an auditable
  evidence-derived reliability policy without changing default behavior.
- This is still a transparent heuristic estimator, not a learned model. Its
  role is to expose the right row-level signals and provide a stronger baseline
  for later calibration or learning.
- On the small balanced3 oracle smoke, evidence mode improved over both the
  fixed-prior memory-guided run (`419` actions) and `naive_count` (`387`
  actions), while keeping all 3 successes. This is encouraging but still only
  a tiny oracle smoke.
- A larger balanced6 evidence-mode oracle navmesh smoke produced
  `memory_guided=565`, `frontier_only=943`, and `naive_count=575` actions,
  with all 6 memory-guided episodes successful. Memory-guided chose memory for
  chair, toilet, bed, and tv_monitor, and frontier for plant and sofa.

Follow-up:
- Run a larger balanced evidence-mode navmesh smoke and then move reliability
  calibration away from oracle pixels toward detector/per-action evidence.

  `memory_rescued_frontier_failure=1`, `frontier_shorter_selected=1`, and
  `valid_memory_wrongly_deferred=1`.

Effect:
- Upcoming Habitat smokes can be interpreted by mechanism rather than only
  aggregate action counts. This should reduce false confidence from tiny gains.
- The balanced6 bucket rerun shows one clear estimator error (`sofa` valid

  scaling claims.

Follow-up:

## 2026-05-29 - Calibrate Strong Positive Memory Reliability

File:

- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`

Change:
- Added regression tests from the balanced6 hindsight-regret smoke: a strong
  valid `sofa` memory at 98 actions should not be deferred to a 102-action
  frontier path, while the `plant` row should still prefer the shorter
  125-action frontier over a 139-action memory route.
- Shifted evidence-mode reliability weighting toward current positive evidence
  and accepted matching, and added a `strong_current_evidence_floor` at `0.96`
  when current evidence is strong, matching is accepted, and transform
  covariance remains low.
- Updated the closed-loop Habitat design doc to describe evidence reliability
  as implemented, including the overfitting risk of this transparent
  calibration.

Reason:
- The balanced6 evidence-mode navmesh smoke had one clear estimator mistake:
  `sofa` was bucketed as `valid_memory_wrongly_deferred` even though hindsight
  showed memory was slightly shorter than frontier. The estimator was
  over-penalizing strong current positive memory because the post-memory
  fallback route was expensive.

  memory case before the estimator change.

  `PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py::test_calibrated_memory_decision_keeps_strong_shorter_memory src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py::test_calibrated_memory_decision_still_uses_frontier_when_frontier_is_shorter src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py::test_evidence_reliability_boosts_strong_current_positive_memory src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py::test_evidence_reliability_rejects_nonpositive_or_ambiguous_memory -q`
  produced `4` passed.
- Focused Habitat/CLI tests:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_cli.py -q`
  produced `36` passed.
- Full local core tests:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests -q`
  produced `222` passed.
- `python -m py_compile src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`
- `git diff --check`
- Linux focused Habitat/CLI tests after pulling commit `3bff3c5` produced
  `36` passed.
- Linux balanced6 oracle navmesh evidence smoke with calibration produced
  `memory_guided=561`, `naive_count=575`, and `frontier_only=943` actions.
  `memory_guided` had bucket counts `memory_shorter_reused=4`,
  `memory_rescued_frontier_failure=1`, and `frontier_shorter_selected=1`, with
  `total_hindsight_action_regret=0`.
- The targeted `sofa` row selected memory at `98` actions and moved to
  `memory_shorter_reused`; the targeted `plant` row still selected frontier at
  `125` actions and stayed `frontier_shorter_selected`.
- Added
  `docs/experiments/2026-05-29-habitat-navmesh-evidence-calibration-smoke.md`
  with the command, selected groups, aggregate metrics, row audit, caveats, and
  follow-up.
- Linux Grounding-DINO candidate-view balanced6 navmesh evidence smoke produced
  `memory_guided=564`, `naive_count=575`, and `frontier_only=946` actions.
  `memory_guided` preserved the same bucket pattern, had
  `total_hindsight_action_regret=0`, selected memory for `sofa`, and selected
  frontier for `plant`.
- Added
  `docs/experiments/2026-05-29-habitat-navmesh-grounding-dino-evidence-calibration-smoke.md`
  with detector-backed row evidence, metrics, caveats, and follow-up.

Effect:
- Evidence mode now treats accepted, strongly visible, low-covariance memory as
  highly reliable enough to survive a slightly cheaper-looking frontier
  comparison. This should remove the balanced6 `sofa` regret row while keeping
  the `plant` shorter-frontier decision.
- This is still a transparent heuristic calibration, not a learned reliability
  model. It is useful for exposing the decision surface and preventing a known
  local mistake, but it is not a publishable estimator by itself.

Follow-up:
- Add weak-evidence and stale-memory Grounding-DINO calibration cases.
- Replace candidate-view oracle reliability with per-action detector evidence
  and evaluate on held-out scenes/categories before using the result for paper

## 2026-05-29 - Remove Oracle Pixels From Detector Reliability

File:
- `docs/design/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-29-habitat-navmesh-grounding-dino-evidence-calibration-smoke.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-29-habitat-navmesh-grounding-dino-stale-detector-pixels-smoke.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`

Change:
- Added a detector-backed reliability regression where detector evidence is
  weak but oracle pixels are large.
- Changed `_current_evidence_reliability_component(...)` so detector-backed
  evidence uses `detector_pixels`, not `max(detector_pixels,
  oracle_target_pixels)`.
- Updated the closed-loop Habitat design doc to state that oracle pixels may be
  logged for audit/gate diagnostics but must not inflate Grounding-DINO-backed
  policy reliability.
- Updated the Grounding-DINO calibration report to the detector-pixel v2 run and
  added a stale detector-pixel report.

Reason:
- While auditing the Grounding-DINO calibration smoke, the reliability helper
  still allowed oracle semantic pixels to boost current evidence in detector
  mode. That is a privileged signal and would weaken any future paper claim.

Verification:
- Red test:
  `PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py::test_detector_reliability_uses_detector_pixels_not_oracle_pixels -q`
  failed with `assert 0.98 == 0.72` before the fix.
- After the fix, the same test produced `1` passed.
- Focused local Habitat/CLI tests produced `37` passed.
- Full local core tests produced `223` passed.
- `python -m py_compile src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`
- `git diff --check`
- Linux focused Habitat/CLI tests after pulling commit `ffcfd41` produced
  `37` passed.
- Linux stable Grounding-DINO balanced6 navmesh detector-pixel v2 smoke
  preserved the previous aggregate result:
  `memory_guided=564`, `naive_count=575`, and `frontier_only=946`, with
  `memory_guided` hindsight action regret `0`. Every selected memory evidence
  row had detector pixels above the strong-evidence threshold.
- Linux stale-proxy Grounding-DINO balanced6 navmesh detector-pixel v2 smoke
  produced `memory_guided=1334`, `naive_count=1962`, and `frontier_only=2038`
  actions across 12 rows. Repeat-0 stale memories had
  `current_evidence=0.15`, `memory_valid_prior=0.225`, and reason
  `matching_no_current_observation`; repaired detector-positive memories could
  return to prior `0.96` on repeat 1.

Effect:
- Detector-backed reliability no longer borrows oracle semantic pixel counts.
  The current stable/stale candidate-view smokes are unchanged in aggregate
  because their selected memory detector masks are already large enough, but the
  fairness boundary is now enforced by a unit test.
- The strong-positive floor is less likely to hide stale old memories because
  stale-proxy non-confirmations keep `shared_gate_success=false` and
  `current_evidence=0.15`.

Follow-up:
- Add cases where Grounding-DINO produces weak-but-positive memory detections
  so the expected-utility boundary is tested below the strong-evidence floor.
- Move detector evidence from selected candidate views into per-action
  observations.

## 2026-05-29 - Add Per-Action Route Observation Mode

File:
- `docs/design/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/run_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_action_follower.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_action_follower.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_cli.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`

Change:
- Added `HabitatRouteObservation` records after every GreedyGeodesic action,
  including action index, action name, pose, rotation, and cumulative distance.
- Added `--route-observation-mode option_end|per_action`; `option_end` remains
  the default to preserve existing candidate/probe endpoint smokes.
- In `per_action`, memory, search-proxy fallback, post-memory fallback, and
  navmesh probe routes are verified in execution order and charged only through
  the first positive shared-gate observation.
- Kept `navmesh_frontier` target-agnostic: it still samples probes from the
  pathfinder and does not use `fallback_candidate.position` as a hidden goal.
- Added a stale-proxy guard so initial stale memory attempts are not truncated
  by synthetic old-object positives; they remain non-confirming and pay the
  original memory route cost.

Reason:
- Candidate endpoint checks can hide that a robot would have seen the target
  earlier or missed it along the way. Per-action route observations are a
  necessary step toward real closed-loop ObjectNav perception while preserving
  the existing option-end baseline for reproducibility.
- Stale memory accounting must not get cheaper because a protocol-synthetic
  old memory pose happens to be visible along the route.

Verification:
- Red tests first failed for missing route observations, missing
  `route_observation_mode`, missing `_observe_route_until_positive`, and missing
  CLI support.
- Added a second red stale-proxy regression; it failed first because
  `_observe_initial_memory_route` was missing, then because stale evidence was
  still positive before the guard forced non-confirmation.
- Focused local tests:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_action_follower.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_cli.py -q`
  produced `46` passed.
- Full local core tests:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests -q`
  produced `229` passed.
- Syntax checks:
  `python -m py_compile src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_action_follower.py src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/run_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`
- `git diff --check`
- Linux focused Habitat route/CLI tests after pulling commit `847c66a`
  produced `46` passed.
- Linux 1-group oracle navmesh smoke with `--route-observation-mode
  per_action` completed successfully. The run recorded
  `route_observation_mode=per_action` and `frontier_mode=navmesh_frontier`.
  On the selected `plant` group, `memory_guided=118` actions,

Effect:

  frontier policy, and no action-by-action replanning.

Follow-up:

## 2026-05-29 - Add Detector Multiview Confirmation Interface

File:
- `docs/design/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`

- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_cli.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`

Change:
- Added `--detector-confirmation-mode single_frame|multiview` plus frame,
  translation, rotation, and mask-IoU thresholds to the Habitat closed-loop CLI
  and summaries. `single_frame` remains the default.
- Added a detector confirmation helper that suppresses raw single-frame
  detector positives to weak `UNKNOWN` evidence until repeated positives have
  sufficient view change and mask consistency.
- Wired confirmation through detector-backed candidate verification,
  per-action route observation, and navmesh frontier probe verification.
- Added row payload and per-policy summary counts for detector confirmation
  outcomes.
- Kept oracle-overlap checks audit-only; the new policy-facing gate uses
  detector repeatability and mask consistency, not privileged semantic overlap.

Reason:
- The balanced3 Grounding-DINO per-action smoke exposed a detector-positive
  `plant` memory with zero semantic overlap. Detector overlap is unavailable on
  the robot, so the next useful interface is a reproducible multiview
  confirmation ablation rather than a hand-tuned oracle overlap rule.

Verification:
- Red preflight test failed because `detector_confirmation_mode` was not an API
  argument.
- Red CLI test failed because detector confirmation flags were unrecognized.
- Red helper tests failed because `DetectorConfirmationState` and
  `DetectorConfirmedVerification` did not exist.
- Red summary test failed because `detector_confirmation_counts` was missing.
- Focused detector confirmation tests produced `5` passed.
- Focused detector confirmation CLI test produced `1` passed.
- Focused Habitat route/CLI tests:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_action_follower.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_cli.py -q`
  produced `58` passed.
- Full local core tests:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests -q`
  produced `241` passed.
- `python -m py_compile src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_memory_lifecycle_objectnav.py src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/run_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`
- `git diff --check`
- Linux focused Habitat route/CLI tests after pulling commit `88d1fa0`
  produced `58` passed.
- Linux 1-group Grounding-DINO per-action navmesh smoke with
  `--detector-confirmation-mode multiview` completed successfully. It recorded
  `detector_confirmation_mode=multiview`, `route_observation_mode=per_action`,
  and `detector=grounding_dino`. The selected `plant` group produced
  `memory_guided=127`, `naive_count=127`, and `frontier_only=354` actions.
  Policy summaries reported `detector_confirmation_counts={'confirmed': 2}`.
  Memory evidence and post-memory fallback evidence were
  `confirmed_detector_positive_mask`; no detector false confirmations were
  counted in this smoke.

Effect:
- Detector-positive memory reuse can now be compared in `single_frame` versus
  `multiview` mode without changing the rest of the Habitat smoke protocol.
- Suppressed detector positives become weak evidence for reliability and do not
  refresh strong memory by themselves.

Follow-up:
- Run paired `single_frame`/`multiview` ablations on the balanced detector
  smoke before claiming any detector-backed improvement.
- Add a targeted weak-positive case, because the first Linux multiview smoke
  confirmed positives but did not exercise suppressed-positive summary counts.

## 2026-05-29 - Run Detector Confirmation Balanced3 Ablation

File:
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-29-habitat-detector-confirmation-ablation-balanced3.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Added an experiment report comparing `single_frame` and `multiview`
  detector-confirmation modes on the same Grounding-DINO per-action navmesh
  balanced3 smoke.

Reason:
- The multiview confirmation interface needs measured behavior before it can be
  treated as a useful detector reliability mechanism. The important question is
  not only whether it removes a false confirmation, but whether stricter
  confirmation harms memoryless frontier exploration.

Verification:
- Linux `single_frame` balanced3 run completed successfully at
  `runs/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor/per_action_grounding_dino_navmesh_balanced3_single_frame_confirm_v1`.
- Linux `multiview` balanced3 run completed successfully at
  `runs/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor/per_action_grounding_dino_navmesh_balanced3_multiview_confirm_v1`.
- Two invalid multiview command attempts failed before producing valid metrics:
  one had a wrong depth-noise path and one had an invalid PyTorch allocator
  option. The report excludes both.
- Parsed both `summary.json` files and compared success counts, action counts,
  detector false-confirmation counts, detector confirmation counts, and row
  evidence reasons.
- `git diff --check`

Effect:
- `single_frame` kept the earlier small action profile
  (`memory_guided=347`, `frontier_only=357`, `naive_count=347`) but retained a
  `plant` memory false confirmation.
- `multiview` removed the false confirmation and produced
  `detector_confirmation_counts={'confirmed': 7}`, but made `frontier_only`
  fail on two categories (`memory_guided=354`, `frontier_only=1016`,
  `naive_count=354`).
- This supports an adaptive or learned confirmation/reliability direction, not
  a fixed claim that multiview confirmation is always better.

Follow-up:
- Add a targeted weak-positive runtime case so suppressed-positive counts are
  observed outside unit tests.
- Scale the paired confirmation ablation across more scenes before using it in
  paper claims.

## 2026-05-29 - Add Adaptive Detector Event Reliability Mode

File:
- `docs/design/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`
- `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-05-29-adaptive-detector-event-reliability.md`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_cli.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`

Change:
- Added `--memory-reliability-mode event_posterior` as an explicit mode beside
  `fixed` and `evidence`.
- Added a context-filtered detector confirmation event posterior that blends
  the existing evidence reliability estimate with confirmed/suppressed
  detector-event evidence.
- Passed runtime `detector_confirmation_events` into the memory reliability
  estimate, using `memory` for original anchors and `fallback_from_memory` for
  repaired stale-memory anchors.
- Recorded the slice in the design doc and implementation plan.

Reason:
- Runtime event diagnostics now expose suppressed detector positives, but the
  policy still used only selected evidence and hand-designed reliability
  components. `event_posterior` is the first policy-facing bridge from raw
  detector-event traces to memory-vs-frontier expected utility without using
  oracle semantic overlap.

Verification:
- Red CLI test failed because `event_posterior` was not an accepted
  `--memory-reliability-mode` choice.
- Red helper tests failed because `_estimate_memory_valid_prior` did not accept
  detector confirmation events or event context.
- Focused new tests for CLI acceptance, confirmed-event boost,
  suppressed-dominant reduction, context filtering, and oracle-audit invariance
  produced `5` passed after implementation.
- Existing reliability and confirmation tests plus the new event-posterior
  tests produced `11` passed.
- Focused Habitat route/CLI suite:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_action_follower.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_cli.py -q`
  produced `65` passed.
- `python -m py_compile src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/run_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`
- Full local core suite:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests -q`
  produced `248` passed.

Effect:
- `event_posterior` raises reliability when confirmed detector events dominate
  and lowers reliability when suppressed events dominate enough to make
  frontier-first cheaper under expected utility.
- Oracle audit fields such as overlap pixels and oracle recall do not affect
  the posterior, preserving the real-robot boundary.
- This remains an interpretable adaptive baseline, not a learned reliability
  model or paper-ready benchmark claim.

Follow-up:
- Pull this commit on Linux and run focused Habitat tests in the `habitat`
  environment.
- Run paired Grounding-DINO smokes comparing `evidence` and `event_posterior`
  on the same per-action navmesh configuration.
- Use the posterior traces to design a learned reliability model once more
  held-out runtime data is available.

  `memory_guided=354`, `naive_count=354`, and `frontier_only=1016` actions.
  `memory_guided` had `0` hindsight regret in both modes.
- `event_posterior` lowered memory-guided reliability from `0.96` in all rows
  to `0.683481` for `chair`, `0.710654` for `plant`, and `0.81947` for
  `toilet`, but no memory-vs-frontier decision flipped.
- Runtime event counts were stable across modes:
  `detector_confirmation_event_counts={'confirmed': 21, 'suppressed': 29}` for
  each policy summary.

Effect:
- The posterior is verified as a confidence calibration signal on balanced3,
  but stable balanced3 is not decision-sensitive enough to show an action
  improvement or regression.

Follow-up:
- Run stale or weak-evidence comparisons where lower event-posterior confidence
  can plausibly change memory-guided selection.
- Analyze decision flips, bucket counts, and hindsight regret before scaling to
  benchmark-style claims.

## 2026-05-29 - Compare Event Posterior on Stale Balanced3 Grounding-DINO

File:
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-29-habitat-event-posterior-stale-balanced3-comparison.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Added a paired stale-proxy balanced3 Grounding-DINO report comparing
  `evidence` and `event_posterior` with `--query-repeats 2`.

Reason:
- Stable balanced3 lowered reliability but did not flip decisions. The next
  smallest decision-sensitive candidate was stale repair, where repeat 0 should
  reject stale memory and repeat 1 can reuse repaired memory.

Verification:
- Linux `evidence` stale run completed successfully at
  `runs/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor/per_action_grounding_dino_navmesh_balanced3_stale_evidence_v1`.
- Linux `event_posterior` stale run completed successfully at
  `runs/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor/per_action_grounding_dino_navmesh_balanced3_stale_event_posterior_v1`.
- Parsed both `summary.json` files. Both modes produced
  `memory_guided=766`, `naive_count=782`, and `frontier_only=1858` actions
  across six rows, with `memory_guided` hindsight regret `0`.
- `event_posterior` preserved the matching gate on stale repeat-0 rows:
  reliability stayed at `0.225` even when detector-event posterior was high for
  `plant` and `toilet`.
- Repaired repeat-1 reliability dropped from `0.96` to `0.730507` for `chair`,
  `0.734908` for `plant`, and `0.734741` for `toilet`, but no decisions
  flipped.

Effect:
- The posterior is safe under stale repair: it does not use detector events to
  override missing current memory evidence.
- This is still not a policy win. It says the next experiment must construct or
  select a weak-evidence, cost-close row where confidence changes can affect
  hindsight regret.

Follow-up:
- Build a decision-sensitive weak-evidence calibration case instead of simply
  scaling the same stable/stale setting.
- Use row-level posterior traces as supervision targets for learned reliability.

## 2026-05-29 - Mine Broad Summaries and Run Balanced6 Event-Posterior Checks

File:
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-29-habitat-decision-sensitivity-broad-balanced6.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Added a broad Linux mining report after the boundary-region refinement.
- Ran a targeted balanced6 Grounding-DINO per-action `event_posterior` slice.
- Ran matched option-end balanced6 `evidence` and `event_posterior` slices with
  multiview confirmation to isolate posterior effects from detector-confirmation
  effects.

Reason:
- Existing balanced3 artifacts had detector-event signal but no flippable
  boundary. Broad mining was needed to find whether existing runs already had
  all ingredients for a reliability-driven policy change.

Verification:
- Broad mining over `runs/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor` completed on Linux:
  `50` summaries, `206` memory-guided rows, `144` candidates, `95`
  reliability-sensitive boundaries, `0` warnings, and `0` counterfactual flips.
- Linux balanced6 per-action Grounding-DINO `event_posterior` run completed at
  `runs/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor/per_action_grounding_dino_navmesh_balanced6_event_posterior_v1`.
  It produced `memory_guided=473`, `naive_count=473`, and `frontier_only=1064`
  actions, with `memory_guided` success `6/6` and `0` hindsight regret.
- The per-action balanced6 mining report found `6` candidate rows and `0`
  flips; none had an interior reliability boundary.
- One option-end event-posterior command failed before metrics because the
  depth-noise profile path was mistyped. It was rerun with a corrected path and
  a new output directory.
- Matched option-end multiview `evidence` and `event_posterior` balanced6 runs
  completed. Both produced identical decisions and aggregates:
  `memory_guided=773`, `naive_count=573`, `frontier_only=2079`; success was
  `5/6`, `5/6`, and `1/6`.
- Matched option-end mining found `0` counterfactual flips for both modes.

Effect:
- The current evidence is a negative result for `event_posterior` policy
  benefit: it calibrates confidence but still does not cross decision
  boundaries in the tested slices.
- The apparent option-end regression versus older evidence reports is due to
  multiview confirmation changing verification behavior, not the posterior rule
  alone.

Follow-up:
- Build a targeted decision-boundary slice with interior reliability boundary,
  mixed detector-event evidence, and enough posterior shift to cross the
  boundary.

## 2026-05-30 - Run Goal-Object Relocation Habitat Smoke

File:
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-30-habitat-goal-object-relocation-smoke.md`
- `docs/design/2026-05-30-habitat-goal-object-relocation-challenge.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Fast-forwarded the Linux Habitat checkout to `43c0193`.
- Ran focused Linux closed-loop/CLI tests after sync.
- Ran two-group oracle and Grounding-DINO relocation smokes.
- Mined both relocation smokes with the decision-sensitivity miner.
- Added an experiment report for the relocation smoke results.

Reason:
- Local unit/preflight checks prove the new code path is wired, but the
  research claim needs real Habitat evidence that relocated groups are
  constructed and evaluated correctly.

Verification:
- Linux focused tests produced `70` passed.
- Oracle relocation smoke completed at
  `runs/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor/oracle_goal_object_relocation_navmesh_smoke_2group_20260530_v1`.
- Grounding-DINO relocation smoke completed at
  `runs/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor/grounding_dino_goal_object_relocation_navmesh_event_posterior_smoke_2group_20260530_v1`.
- Oracle mining completed at
  `runs/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor/decision_sensitivity_oracle_goal_object_relocation_smoke_2group_20260530_v1/report.json`.
- Grounding-DINO mining completed at
  `runs/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor/decision_sensitivity_grounding_dino_goal_object_relocation_smoke_2group_20260530_v1/report.json`.

Effect:
- Both smokes selected the same real relocated pairs:
  `bed goal_object:194->goal_object:698` and
  `chair goal_object:13->goal_object:483`.
- Oracle `memory_guided` avoided harmful stale-memory reuse for both rows and
  succeeded on one of two rows; `naive_count` reused stale memory and succeeded
  on zero of two rows.
- Grounding-DINO `memory_guided` also avoided stale-memory reuse, but all two
  detector rows failed under this small smoke configuration.
- Mining found reliability-sensitive relocation rows, but `0` counterfactual
  flips. Grounding-DINO event posterior stayed at `0.2875` because the runtime
  confirmation events were fallback-context events, not memory-validity events.

Follow-up:
- Run a broader relocation sweep and select pairs by boundary interval gap,
  fallback success, and old/new separation before claiming detector-event
  reliability improvements.
