## 2026-05-30 - Run Ranked Relocation Sweep

File:
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-30-habitat-goal-object-relocation-smoke.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Fast-forwarded Linux to `d8db80d`.
- Ran the focused closed-loop/miner tests on Linux.
- Ran ranked balanced6 oracle and Grounding-DINO relocation sweeps.
- Mined both ranked sweeps and updated the relocation experiment report.

Reason:
- The selector change should be judged by whether it actually picks harder
  old/new pairs and improves decision-boundary geometry in Habitat artifacts.

Verification:
- Linux focused tests produced `70` passed.
- Oracle ranked sweep completed at
  `runs/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor/oracle_goal_object_relocation_ranked_navmesh_balanced6_20260530_v1`.
- Oracle ranked mining completed at
  `runs/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor/decision_sensitivity_oracle_goal_object_relocation_ranked_balanced6_20260530_v1/report.json`.
- Grounding-DINO ranked sweep completed at
  `runs/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor/grounding_dino_goal_object_relocation_ranked_navmesh_event_posterior_balanced6_20260530_v1`.
- Grounding-DINO ranked mining completed at
  `runs/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor/decision_sensitivity_grounding_dino_goal_object_relocation_ranked_balanced6_20260530_v1/report.json`.

Effect:
- Ranked oracle selection now includes separation distances up to about
  `19.55 m` and finds a near-boundary `toilet` relocation row with
  `boundary_reliability=0.309783`, `actual_reliability=0.2875`, and
  `boundary_reliability_interval_gap=0.022283`.
- Ranked Grounding-DINO mining found `4` reliability-sensitive rows, including
  the same `toilet` target with gap `0.048397`, but still `0` flips.
- Detector runtime events existed, but memory-validity event counts remained
  `0` because the events were fallback-context observations, not pre-decision
  memory validation evidence.

Follow-up:
- The next algorithmic question is not more random sweeping; it is how to get
  pre-decision memory-context detector evidence or a learned validity model
  that can move reliability on rows like the ranked `toilet` case.

## 2026-05-30 - Add Memory-Validity Dataset Exporter

Change:
- Added an offline exporter that turns Habitat closed-loop `summary.json` rows
  into supervised memory-validity examples.
- Labels come from `memory_evidence.shared_gate_success`.
- Features are restricted to planned costs, memory evidence, reliability
  components, memory-context detector-event components, and relocation
  distance. Future fallback evidence and row success are intentionally excluded
  from features.
- Added a CLI for JSON and CSV export.

Reason:
- Ranked relocation artifacts exposed near-boundary rows, but event posterior
  does not move when no pre-decision memory-context events exist. The next
  paper-grade step is a learned/calibrated validity model, not another
  hand-weighted reliability rule.

## 2026-05-30 - Add Memory-Validity Logistic Baseline

Change:
- Added a deterministic offline logistic-regression baseline for exported
  Habitat memory-validity datasets.
- The trainer uses only exporter feature fields, imputes missing values from
  training means, standardizes features, and reports accuracy, log loss, Brier
  score, and label counts.
- Added a CLI that writes a self-contained JSON model report.

Reason:
- The relocation benchmark now supplies stale-memory supervision, but
  hand-tuned event posterior evidence still has not produced detector-backed
  pre-decision policy flips. A learned validity baseline is the next auditable
  step toward calibrated reliability without adding another rule constant.

  builds an exporter-compatible pre-decision feature dictionary, predicts
  learned memory validity, and uses that probability in expected-utility
  memory-vs-frontier selection.
- Added CLI support for `--memory-validity-model` and summary metadata for the
  model path.

Reason:
- Offline learned scoring produced a detector-backed validity flip on a
  relocated `sofa` row, but the project still needed an actual Habitat runner
  path before that could count as an online policy decision.

Verification:
- Red helper test failed before `_apply_learned_memory_validity_model` existed.
- Red CLI test failed before `--memory-validity-model` was accepted.
- Focused helper, feature-name parity, and CLI tests passed after
  implementation.
- Focused Habitat runner/CLI tests:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_cli.py -q`
  produced `74` passed.
- Focused learning-pipeline/model/exporter/miner tests produced `21` passed.
- `python -m py_compile src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/run_habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor_objectnav.py`
- Full local core suite:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests -q`
  produced `282` passed.
- `git diff --check`
- Pushed commit `6162b8a` and pulled it on Linux.
- Linux focused Habitat runner/CLI tests produced `74` passed.
- Linux evidence-only learning pipeline on stable-plus-relocation
  Grounding-DINO artifacts produced `12` examples with `5` valid and `7`
  invalid labels, `1` learned decision flip, and `0` heuristic
  decision-sensitivity flips.
- Linux matched online selected-group replay confirmed the baseline
  event-posterior row chose `memory_first` at reliability `0.2875`, while the
  learned-model row chose `frontier_first` at probability `0.006685`.
- Linux broader matched current-code replays produced one learned online flip in
  stable balanced6 (`tv_monitor`) and one learned online flip in relocation
  balanced6 (`sofa`), but no success improvement. Stable memory-guided stayed
  at `4/6` success and action count worsened from `528` to `795`; relocation
  stayed at `0/6` success and worsened from `1446` to `1643` actions.
- The current-code evidence-only training pipeline wrote
  `runs/habitat_closed_loop_dual_anchor/memory_validity_learning_grounding_dino_current_stable_relocation_balanced6_evidence_only_20260530_v1`
  and produced a model JSON identical to the earlier evidence-only model.

Effect:
- A learned memory-validity model can now be replayed through the same Habitat
  route accounting and row summaries used by fixed/evidence/event-posterior
  baselines.
- The project now has an actual online Habitat policy flip from learned
  pre-decision validity, but the selected relocation row still fails under both
  baseline and learned replay.
- Broader evidence says learned validity is now a working mechanism but not yet
  a benchmark gain; the next bottleneck is target-agnostic frontier/search
  recovery after stale-memory rejection.

Follow-up:
- Improve frontier/search so stale-memory avoidance can become a success or
  efficiency gain, then rerun the current-code matched learned matrix.

## 2026-05-30 - Run Per-Action Route Observation Matrix

File:
- `docs/design/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-30-habitat-per-action-route-observation-matrix.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Ran matched current-code `per_action` route-observation replays for stable
  balanced6 and goal-object relocation balanced6.
- Compared event-posterior reliability against the evidence-only learned
  memory-validity model under the same per-action route accounting.
- Recorded that per-action route observation improves success, while learned
  validity is neutral in the matched per-action matrix.

Reason:
- The previous learned-validity online matrix proved policy flips, but did not
  improve success because endpoint-only frontier/search was weak. The next
  question was whether route-level observations along actual fallback routes
  can turn stale-memory rejection or memory-conditioned search into recovery.

Verification:
- Linux selected relocated `sofa` per-action baseline and learned replays both
  succeeded in `171` actions, while matched `option_end` replays failed.
- Linux relocation balanced6 per-action event-posterior and learned replays
  both produced memory-guided `3/6` success and `1176` actions; frontier-only
  produced `2/6` success and `1647` actions.
- Linux stable balanced6 per-action event-posterior and learned replays
  initially produced memory-guided `5/6` success and `441` actions; after the
  unavailable-frontier fix, post-fix reruns produced `6/6` success and `473`
  actions, while frontier-only stayed at `3/6` success and `831` actions.
- A first stable per-action command failed immediately due to a mistyped
  depth-noise profile path; the corrected run used a new `_v2` output.
- `git diff --check`

Effect:
- The most promising current mechanism is route-level active confirmation:
  memory is useful as a spatial prior for local search after stale verification,
  not only as a direct terminal goal.
- The learned-validity hook remains useful as a decision mechanism, but it did
  not add performance once per-action route observation was enabled on this
  balanced6 matrix.

Follow-up:
- Investigate the stable `bed` diagnostic where expected utility selects a
  zero-action failed frontier despite positive memory evidence.
- Scale per-action route observation beyond balanced6 and replace deterministic
  navmesh probes with a real occupancy/depth frontier policy.

## 2026-05-30 - Probe Relocation Search Budget

File:
- `docs/design/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-30-habitat-per-action-route-observation-matrix.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Ran selected high-budget per-action replays on the remaining relocation
  failures with `frontier_probe_count=8` and `frontier_probe_heading_count=4`.
- Matched event-posterior and learned validity on the relocated `tv_monitor`
  row.
- Ran event-posterior high-budget replay on the failed `toilet` row and the
  reverse `chair` relocation row.

Reason:
- The balanced6 relocation matrix showed that per-action route observation
  improves recovery, but still leaves three memory-guided failures. The next
  question was whether those failures are simply navmesh budget limits or
  require a more directed memory-conditioned active-search policy.

Verification:
- High-budget `tv_monitor` event-posterior and learned selected replays both
  succeeded in `684` memory-guided actions; frontier-only succeeded in `998`
  actions.
- High-budget reverse `chair` relocation succeeded in `190` memory-guided
  actions.
- High-budget `toilet` relocation still failed after `803` memory-guided
  actions.

Effect:
- Brute-force navmesh budget can recover some remaining relocation failures,
  but it is too expensive and still incomplete.
- The next algorithmic target should be directed memory-conditioned local
  active search, not just larger random probe budgets.

Follow-up:
- Write a design for memory-conditioned local active search that uses the
  memory anchor as a spatial prior while selecting route observations more
  intelligently than random navmesh probes.

## 2026-05-30 - Add Official Target-Agnostic Frontier Baseline

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py`

Change:
- Added `frontier_only` to the official Habitat ObjectNav adapter.
- Refactored the official loop so policies choose one action from each current
  observation instead of receiving a precomputed action list.
- Implemented the first target-agnostic depth-reactive frontier baseline:
  move forward through clear center depth, keep turning when blocked, and stop
  on budget.
- Added regression coverage for normalized Habitat depth and blocked-turn
  oscillation.

Reason:
- The official adapter needed a real no-memory comparison policy before the
  Dual-Anchor memory policy can be evaluated with official SR/SPL.
- The baseline must not use target pose, route follower shortcuts, semantic
  oracle visibility, detector-positive target shortcuts, or teleportation.

Verification:
- Red tests first failed because `frontier_only` was unsupported.
- Red normalized-depth regression failed because Habitat depth is normalized to
  `0.0..1.0`, not meters.
- Red blocked-turn regression failed because alternating left/right turns
  oscillated in place.
- Focused local tests now produce `10` passed.
- Linux focused tests in conda env `habitat` produce `10` passed.
- Linux official `frontier_only` smoke:
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/frontier_only_valmini_3ep_200steps_turnfix_20260530_v1`.

Effect:
- The official evaluation path can now run a nontrivial target-agnostic baseline
  through `habitat.Env` and record official metrics.
- The first baseline is intentionally weak: `0/3` success, `0.0` SPL, and
  `0.0013203695130148407` SoftSPL on the three-episode `val_mini` smoke.

Follow-up:
- Replace this depth-reactive scaffold with a real occupancy/depth frontier
  mapper or learned exploration policy.
- Then connect `dual_anchor_memory` to the same official action loop and
  compare memory against the official no-memory baseline.

Change:

  ObjectNav baseline.

  observations.
- Added helper coverage for normalized depth projection, free/occupied cell
  marking, frontier counts, and policy debug telemetry.
- Added CLI coverage that `--policy occupancy_frontier` is recorded as a
  target-agnostic occupancy-frontier baseline.
- Added blocked-turn policy coverage so the occupancy baseline keeps turning
  consistently toward the selected frontier when the current depth window is
  blocked.

  Habitat smoke showed frame-by-frame frontier replanning could still alternate
  left/right while blocked.

  counts when this policy is active.

- The first `frontier_only` policy was a weak depth-reactive scaffold. A
  publishable memory comparison needs a fair no-memory baseline that explores
  from observations without target-pose or route-follower shortcuts.

  exposed that `H x W x 1` can squeeze to one dimension.
- Focused local adapter/CLI tests after the coverage update produced `15`
  passed.
- Red compass-change regression failed with `turn_left`, then `turn_right`
  during a blocked span before the turn-burst fix.
- Focused local adapter/CLI tests after the turn-burst fix produced `16`
  passed.

Effect:
- The official evaluation path now has a map-backed no-memory baseline and
  debug telemetry for free, occupied, unknown, and frontier cell counts.
- The first three-episode smoke remains weak: `0/3` success and `0.0` SPL, but
  SoftSPL improved from the depth-reactive scaffold's
  `0.0013203695130148407` to `0.04420002662118805`.
- A full blocked-turn latch was tested in
  `occupancy_frontier_valmini_3ep_200steps_turn_latch_20260530_v3` and is a
  negative debug result: it removed immediate alternation but regressed SoftSPL
  back to `0.0013203695130148407` by over-rotating.

Follow-up:
- Add detector-backed stop decisions so official policies can actually claim
  success when the target is visible.
- Connect Dual-Anchor memory as a bias over the occupancy frontier map, rather
  than as a route-follower shortcut.

Change:
- Added a JSON memory-prior parser and selector for official ObjectNav runs.
- Added `memory_guided_frontier` to the official Habitat action loop.
- Added CLI/config flags for `--memory-prior-path`,
  `--memory-stop-radius-m`, `--memory-bearing-tolerance-deg`, and
  `--memory-min-confidence`.
- The memory policy uses only official `gps`, `compass`, and `depth`
  observations plus an external memory anchor file. It emits only official
  discrete actions and falls back to `occupancy_frontier` when memory is absent
  or blocked.
- Protocol manifests mark synthetic/unvalidated memory priors as invalid for
  benchmark claims.

Reason:
- The official adapter had no way to consume lifelong memory. This slice adds
  the missing official-loop boundary for remembered anchors without using
  route followers, target poses, semantic oracle masks, or teleportation.

Verification:
- Red parser tests first failed because `load_official_memory_prior` did not
  exist.
- Red CLI/config tests failed because `memory_guided_frontier` and memory flags
  were unsupported.
- Red policy tests failed because the episode loop could not receive memory
  anchors.
- Focused local official adapter/CLI tests produced `26` passed.
- Linux focused official adapter/CLI tests in conda env `habitat` produced
  `26` passed.
- Linux official synthetic-prior smoke:
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/memory_guided_frontier_synthetic_prior_1ep_20260530_v1`.

Effect:
- Official Habitat ObjectNav runs can now exercise memory-conditioned actions
  and debug telemetry while still using Habitat-provided SR/SPL metrics.
- The first smoke is intentionally not a performance result: it used a
  synthetic start anchor, emitted one `stop`, and got `0.0` success/SPL.

Follow-up:
- Generate memory priors from a documented non-oracle discovery process.
- Add detector-backed stop/verification so correct memories can produce
  official success instead of only coordinate-based stopping.
- Compare documented-memory `memory_guided_frontier` against
  `occupancy_frontier` on `val_mini`, then scale only if the mechanism gives a
  substantial and explainable gain.

- `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-05-30-lifecycle-memory-prior-export.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Added a read-only SQLite exporter that converts lifecycle
  `object_instance_anchors` plus optional `usability_beliefs` rows into the
  official memory-prior JSON schema.
- Added `objectnav_export_lifecycle_memory_prior` CLI plumbing with filters
  for dataset version, scene id, categories, confidence, source tag, and
  coordinate frame.
- Confidence defaults to the product
  `p_existence * p_location_valid * p_usable` when beliefs are present, and
  `1.0` when an anchor has no belief row.
- Added compact/full Habitat scene-key compatibility for memory selection.
- Added explicit `coordinate_frame` handling. Lifecycle DB exports default to
  `habitat_world`; the official action selector only consumes
  `episode_start_relative` anchors by default.

Reason:
- The prior memory-policy smoke used hand-written synthetic anchors. The
  project needed a reproducible bridge from real lifecycle memory artifacts to
  official evaluator inputs.
- During review, the coordinate-frame mismatch became clear: lifecycle anchors
  are Habitat world coordinates while official `gps` observations are
  episode-start-relative. Acting on them directly would create an invalid
  memory signal, so this slice preserves the artifact while blocking policy
  use until a valid transform or episode-relative logger exists.

Verification:
- Local focused exporter/official tests:
  `31` passed.
- Local full test suite:
  `322` passed.
- Local syntax check:
  `PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python -m compileall -q src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core src/objectnav_core/tests`
  returned cleanly.
- Local whitespace check:
  `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Linux focused exporter/official tests in conda env `habitat`:
  `31` passed.
- Linux real export:
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/lifecycle_memory_prior_export_grounding_dino_detector_anchor_matrix_20260530_v1/official_memory_prior.json`
  with `12` anchors, `12` `habitat_world` frames, and confidence value
  `0.903168`.
- Linux selector probe confirmed default selection returns `None` for the
  exported world-frame anchor, while explicit `habitat_world` inspection
  selects
  `lifecycle_grounding_dino_detector_anchor_matrix:goal_object:445`.
- Linux official one-episode guard smoke:
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/memory_guided_frontier_world_prior_guard_1ep_20260530_v1`.
  It loaded `12` candidates, recorded
  `fallback_reason=no_matching_memory`, and reported Habitat-provided metrics:
  success `0.0`, SPL `0.0`, SoftSPL `0.003961108539044522`.

Effect:
- Real lifecycle memory DBs can now be exported into the official memory-prior
  schema for audit and future use.
- Exported lifecycle priors are intentionally not actionable by the current
  official policy because they are world-frame coordinates.
- The benchmark-facing bottleneck is now explicit: implement a fair
  world-to-episode transform or produce episode-relative memory directly from
  official observations.

Follow-up:
- Design the frame bridge before any official memory-performance comparison
  using lifecycle anchors.
- Add detector-backed stop/verification after actionable memory anchors exist.
- Compare against `occupancy_frontier` only once memory source, coordinate
  frame, and detector verification are all documented.

## 2026-05-30 - Correct Official GPS/Compass Frame Conversion

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py`
- `docs/design/2026-05-30-official-gps-compass-frame-correction.md`
- `docs/design/2026-05-30-official-memory-prior-objectnav-policy.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-30-habitat-official-gps-compass-frame-correction.md`
- `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-05-30-official-gps-compass-frame-correction.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Added regression tests for Habitat GPS component order and compass sign in
  the official `memory_guided_frontier` action path.
- Fixed `_observation_xz` to convert Habitat 2D GPS from `[forward, right]`
  into the adapter's internal `(x=right, z=forward)` frame.
- Fixed `_observation_heading` to negate raw Habitat compass because right
  turns decrease the official compass value.
- Documented that `episode_start_relative` memory priors use `x_m` as right
  and `z_m` as forward, even though Habitat's raw 2D GPS emits forward first.

Reason:
- A live Linux Habitat probe showed one `move_forward` changed GPS from
  approximately `[0.0, 0.0]` to `[0.0444, 0.0]`, and one `turn_right` changed
  compass to approximately `-0.5236`.
- Habitat-Lab source confirms `EpisodicGPSSensor` returns
  `[-agent_position[2], agent_position[0]]`.
- The old adapter boundary interpreted GPS as direct `(x, z)` and used raw
  compass sign, rotating official memory and occupancy geometry away from the
  actual task sensor frame.

Verification:
- RED local regression tests failed before the fix:
  - GPS-order test got `turn_left` instead of `move_forward`.
  - Compass-sign test got `turn_right` instead of `turn_left`.
- GREEN local regression tests passed after the fix.
- Local focused official/exporter tests: `33` passed.
- Linux focused official/exporter tests in conda env `habitat`: `33` passed.
- Linux official forward-anchor smoke:
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/memory_guided_frontier_episode_frame_forward_probe_1ep_20260530_v1`.
  It emitted five `move_forward` actions then `stop`, with memory
  `bearing_error_rad=1.0362705893385282e-07`.
- Linux corrected occupancy-frontier rerun:
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/occupancy_frontier_valmini_3ep_200steps_framefix_20260530_v5`.
  It produced `0/3` success, SPL `0.0`, SoftSPL
  `0.044200068701652974`, and mean distance-to-goal
  `6.509775638580322`.

Effect:
- Official memory-prior and occupancy-frontier geometry now shares the same
  episode-local frame as Habitat's official sensors.
- Previous official smoke results for `occupancy_frontier` should be treated as
  superseded diagnostic scaffolding; the frame-corrected `v5` artifact is now
  the current no-memory scaffold.

Follow-up:
- Build the episode-relative actionable-memory bridge on top of the corrected
  observation boundary.

Change:
- Added `estimate_episode_detection_anchor`, a non-oracle projection helper
  that converts an official observation plus detector bbox into an
  `OfficialMemoryAnchor` with `coordinate_frame="episode_start_relative"`.
- The projection uses corrected official `gps` and `compass` helpers,
  median depth inside the bbox, normalized-depth conversion, bbox horizontal
  bearing, and the internal `x=right, z=forward` frame.
- Added `make_official_memory_prior_payload` so projected anchors can be
  serialized into the existing official memory-prior JSON shape.

Reason:
- The previous bridge could export lifecycle DB memories, but those were
  Habitat world-frame anchors and must remain non-actionable.
- A real memory system also needs the opposite direction: when the robot sees
  an object through an official detector/RGB-D observation, it should be able
  to write an actionable episode-relative memory without oracle target pose or
  Habitat pathfinder data.

Verification:
- RED test run failed with missing module
  `objectnav_core.evaluation.official_episode_memory`.
- GREEN focused projection tests: `6` passed locally.
- Local focused official-memory set: `39` passed.
- Local full test suite: `330` passed.
- Local `compileall` and `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Linux focused official-memory set in conda env `habitat`: `39` passed.
- Linux `git diff --check` returned cleanly.

Effect:
- The official memory path now has a perception-to-memory geometry primitive:
  detector outputs can become `episode_start_relative` anchors that
  `memory_guided_frontier` is allowed to consume.
- This is still not a full detector-backed official memory benchmark. It is
  the tested geometry bridge that the detector integration should call next.

Follow-up:
- Plug this projection into a detector-backed official discovery pass that
  writes memory-prior JSON artifacts from actual RGB-D frames.
- Add detector-backed stop confirmation so memory-guided policy can claim
  success based on target visibility, not only anchor proximity.

## 2026-05-30 - Add Official Detector Memory Discovery Loop

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_official_memory_discovery.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_memory_discovery.py`
- `docs/design/2026-05-30-official-detector-memory-discovery-loop.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-30-official-detector-memory-discovery-loop-smoke.md`
- `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-05-30-official-detector-memory-discovery-loop.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Added `run_habitat_official_memory_discovery`, a detector-injected official
  observation loop that writes `memory_prior.json`, `summary.json`, and
  `detections.csv`.
- The loop calls `detector_adapter.detect(rgb)`, filters detections by current
  episode category and confidence, projects kept detections with
  `estimate_episode_detection_anchor`, and labels anchors as
  `coordinate_frame="episode_start_relative"`.
- Projected candidates are sorted by confidence before applying
  `max_anchors_per_episode`, so the cap keeps stronger detections instead of
  detector order.
- Added an integration test that feeds a generated discovery prior into
  `memory_guided_frontier` and verifies the policy acts on it without falling
  back.

Reason:
- The official memory bridge needed a non-oracle discovery side: memory should
  come from detector positives on official RGB-D observations, not hand-authored
  or lifecycle world-frame anchors.
- The first slice intentionally keeps detector construction outside the module
  so tests can inject deterministic adapters and real detector wiring can be
  added separately.

Verification:
- RED discovery test initially failed with missing module
  `objectnav_core.evaluation.habitat_official_memory_discovery`.
- RED confidence-cap regression failed with the old detector-order behavior:
  exported confidence `0.2` instead of `0.9`.
- GREEN focused discovery tests: `5` passed locally.
- Local focused official-memory set: `44` passed.
- Local full test suite: `335` passed.
- Local `compileall` and `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Linux focused official-memory set in conda env `habitat`: `44` passed.
- Linux `git diff --check` returned cleanly.

Effect:
- The official memory path now has a tested discovery artifact generator:
  injected detector outputs can become actionable episode-relative memory
  priors.
- This is still not a benchmark result. It has not run a real detector over a
  Habitat discovery/query split, and it does not prove improved ObjectNav
  performance.

Follow-up:
- Add CLI/model-factory wiring for Grounding DINO or YOLO-World discovery
  runs.
- Run an official discovery/query smoke with generated priors, then compare
  against corrected `occupancy_frontier` only under a documented protocol.
- Add detector-backed stop confirmation before making stronger memory-policy
  claims.

## 2026-05-30 - Add Official Detector-Confirmed Memory Stop

Change:
- Added optional `target_detector_adapter` and
  `target_detector_min_confidence` parameters to the official ObjectNav
  evaluator and episode loop.
- `memory_guided_frontier` now checks current official RGB through the injected
  detector before memory steering. If a high-confidence detector label matches
  the current episode target category, the policy records
  `decision="stop_on_detector"` and returns Habitat `stop`.
- Added tests proving a matching detector output stops immediately and
  wrong-category or low-confidence detections preserve existing memory
  behavior.

Reason:
- Generated YOLO memory priors were actionable, but the nearest-anchor query
  policy produced a negative result because it could only stop at a remembered
  coordinate. A publishable memory system needs a current-view confirmation
  interface before stronger query policies can be evaluated honestly.

Verification:

  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/memory_guided_frontier_yolo_discovery_prior_detector_stop_4ep_50steps_20260530_v1`.

  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/memory_guided_frontier_yolo_discovery_prior_detector_stop_trace_4ep_50steps_20260530_v1`.
  It recorded `196` detector calls, `0` target-match calls, and `0`
  target-match detections.

Effect:
- The official query loop can now use current-view detector evidence to decide
  STOP without semantic oracle data, Habitat pathfinder shortcuts, or
  non-official metrics.
- The first YOLO query smoke is negative: success stayed `0/4`, SPL stayed
  `0.0`, and SoftSPL stayed `0.0009902771347611306`.
- This localizes the next bottleneck to memory-conditioned search and
  retrieval quality. The hook fired in tests, but the live policy never reached
  a target-detected state.

Follow-up:
- Add reusable detector trace logging for query runs.
- Replace nearest-anchor steering with a stronger memory-conditioned local
  active search policy.
- Add memory fusion/deduplication and evidence accumulation before scaling to
  broader official comparisons.

## 2026-05-30 - Add Official Memory-Belief Frontier Policy

File:

Change:
- Added `memory_belief_frontier`, an official query policy that scores local
  occupancy frontiers using a Gaussian target belief around the selected memory
  anchor minus travel cost.
- The policy preserves detector-confirmed STOP and built-in detector tracing,
  then falls back to occupancy frontier when no matching memory or scored
  frontier exists.
- Added focused tests for scoring behavior, policy registration, debug payload,
  and detector STOP precedence.

Reason:
- The nearest-anchor memory consumer failed to generate target-view detector
  evidence. This policy is the first step toward memory-conditioned active
  search: memory becomes a target belief over frontier candidates rather than a
  single coordinate to point at.

Verification:
- RED helper test failed because `_select_memory_belief_frontier` was missing.
- RED policy tests failed because `memory_belief_frontier` was unsupported.

- Local `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Linux focused official-memory/exporter set in conda env `habitat`: `58`
  passed.
- Linux `compileall` returned cleanly.
- Linux `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Linux YOLO query smoke wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/memory_belief_frontier_yolo_discovery_prior_detector_trace_4ep_50steps_20260530_v1`.

Effect:
- The diagnostic smoke is not a benchmark win: official success stayed `0/4`,
  SPL stayed `0.0`, and SoftSPL stayed `0.0009902771347611306`.
- It did improve detector evidence relative to nearest-anchor memory steering:
  built-in trace recorded `1` target-match detection instead of `0`.
- The next bottleneck is range-aware detector confirmation/approach. A matching
  label alone can trigger STOP before Habitat success is satisfied.

Follow-up:
- Add detector-guided approach/centering before STOP.
- Add memory fusion/deduplication to create better target belief distributions.
- Re-run official smokes only as diagnostics until success/SPL improve.

## 2026-05-30 - Add Official Detector-Guided Memory Approach

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py`

Change:
- Changed `memory_belief_frontier` so current-view target detections become
  local control evidence instead of unconditional STOP.
- Added detector evidence fields for bbox center offset, bbox area fraction,
  bbox depth median, and whether the depth frame appears normalized.
- Added range-aware STOP gating:
  - off-center target detections turn toward the bbox;
  - centered far detections move forward when the depth corridor is clear;
  - STOP is emitted only for centered, sufficiently large, close-depth target
    detections.
- Preserved `memory_guided_frontier` immediate detector-confirmed STOP behavior
  for compatibility.

Reason:
- The previous `memory_belief_frontier` smoke improved target-view evidence but
  still failed official success because any matching detector label triggered
  STOP, even when the agent was likely too far or off-center.

  immediate STOP on matching target labels.

  close-depth STOP, and `memory_guided_frontier` compatibility.

  passed.

- Linux `git diff --check` returned cleanly.

  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/memory_belief_frontier_detector_guided_approach_yolo_discovery_prior_detector_trace_4ep_50steps_20260530_v1`.

Effect:
- This is not a benchmark improvement: official success stayed `0/4`, SPL
  stayed `0.0`, and SoftSPL stayed `0.0009902771347611306`.
- Detector evidence improved substantially: built-in trace recorded `196`
  detector calls, `257` detections, `23` target-match calls, and `23`
  target-match detections.
- The target episode ended with `decision=center_detector_target`, not STOP,
  so the premature STOP failure was reduced but the local control/search policy
  still did not solve the episode.

Follow-up:

  diagnosed directly.

  visual-servo state.

Change:
- Added per-episode detector center-direction state to `memory_belief_frontier`.
- If a target detection is lost immediately after a detector-centering action,
  the policy now flips the center-direction sign and emits
  `decision=reacquire_detector_target` for one step.
- The next target detection uses the flipped center-direction sign.
- Added a sequence-detector test reproducing target seen, target lost, target
  seen again, proving the policy flips direction after immediate loss.

Reason:
- The policy trace showed a two-step loop: detector centering turned one way,
  then fallback reversed it and recovered the same right-edge target view. The
  adaptive servo tested whether one-step action-effect memory was enough to
  break that loop.

Verification:
- RED adaptive-servo test failed because no-match steps after detector
  centering still fell through to fallback.

  and debug fields.

  passed.

  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/memory_belief_frontier_adaptive_servo_yolo_discovery_prior_detector_trace_4ep_50steps_20260530_v1`.

Effect:
- This was not a benchmark improvement: official success stayed `0/4`, SPL
  stayed `0.0`, and SoftSPL stayed `0.0009902771347611306`.
- The servo converted `22` fallback reversals into
  `reacquire_detector_target` decisions but did not change action counts or
  solve the episode.
- The result argues against more one-step rules. The next policy needs
  multi-frame evidence accumulation or a learned local visual-servo/search
  controller.

Follow-up:
- Design a multi-frame detector-evidence controller with bounded search state,
  not another immediate flip.
- Use `policy_trace.json` as the training/debug artifact for learned local
  control.

Change:
- Added `detector_center_direction_sign` to the official ObjectNav run config,
  preflight/eval functions, episode loop, protocol manifest, and CLI.
- The default remains `1`, preserving existing behavior. The opt-in value
  `-1` initializes detector centering with the opposite bbox-offset-to-turn
  mapping.
- Added tests proving the inverted sign changes the first off-axis detector
  centering action and is recorded through CLI preflight artifacts.

Reason:
- The adaptive detector servo negative result left one plausible root-cause
  hypothesis: the live Habitat camera/action convention could have the opposite
  sign from the controller's bbox centering assumption. This slice tests that
  hypothesis explicitly instead of silently changing policy behavior.

Verification:
- RED episode-loop test failed because
  `detector_center_direction_sign` was not accepted.
- RED CLI test failed because `--detector-center-direction-sign` was not a
  recognized argument.
- Targeted RED/GREEN tests: `2` passed.
- Local official evaluator/CLI tests: `47` passed.
- Local focused official-memory/exporter set: `66` passed.
- Local `compileall` returned cleanly.
- Local `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Linux focused official-memory/exporter set in conda env `habitat`: `66`
  passed.
- Linux `compileall` returned cleanly.
- Linux `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Linux YOLO diagnostic wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/memory_belief_frontier_inverted_center_sign_yolo_discovery_prior_detector_trace_4ep_50steps_20260530_v1`.

Effect:
- The sign ablation is negative: official success stayed `0/4`, SPL stayed
  `0.0`, and SoftSPL stayed `0.0009902771347611306`.
- Detector evidence was unchanged from adaptive servo:
  `196` detector calls, `224` detections, and `23` target-match detections.
- Policy trace shape was unchanged: `23` detector-centering decisions, `22`
  reacquisition decisions, and `148` fallback decisions.
- The first target-control action changed direction, but the policy still
  alternated around the same target-visible heading. This argues against the
  simple image-sign bug hypothesis.

Follow-up:
- Stop investing in one-step centering sign/flip rules unless a new trace
  provides stronger evidence.
- Design a multi-frame detector-evidence controller or learned local visual
  servo that can own local search across brief target dropouts.

## 2026-05-30 - Add Official Detector Action-Effect Local Control

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py`

Change:
- Added the official policy variant `memory_evidence_frontier`.
- Added per-episode failed detector-centering action-effect memory.
- If a centering turn immediately loses a target and reacquisition returns to
  the same edge-visible target, the new policy suppresses the failed centering
  action and emits `approach_detector_target_after_center_loss` when the
  forward corridor is clear.
- Kept `memory_belief_frontier` behavior unchanged for previous ablations.

Reason:
- The adaptive-servo and sign-ablation smokes showed that one-step flip/sign
  rules only mirror the oscillation. The target can be visible at one heading
  while either 30-degree turn loses it, so the policy needs online
  action-effect evidence rather than another fixed centering convention.

Verification:
- RED registration tests failed because `memory_evidence_frontier` was not
  supported by the evaluator or CLI.
- RED behavior test failed because the new policy still delegated to
  `memory_belief_frontier` and repeated centering after reacquisition.
- Targeted RED/GREEN tests: `3` passed.
- Local official evaluator tests: `44` passed.
- Local official CLI tests: `6` passed.
- Local focused official-memory/exporter set: `69` passed.
- Local `compileall` returned cleanly.
- Local `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Linux focused official-memory/exporter set in conda env `habitat`: `69`
  passed.
- Linux `compileall` returned cleanly.
- Linux `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Linux YOLO diagnostic wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/memory_evidence_frontier_action_effect_yolo_discovery_prior_detector_trace_4ep_50steps_20260530_v1`.

Effect:
- This is not an official benchmark win: success stayed `0/4` and SPL stayed
  `0.0`.
- SoftSPL improved from `0.0009902771347611306` to
  `0.02518699682786324`; mean distance-to-goal improved from
  `5.880594372749329` to `5.697803378105164`.
- The target episode loop was largely removed:
  `center_detector_target` dropped from `23` to `1`, and
  `reacquire_detector_target` dropped from `22` to `1`.
- The new policy produced `3`
  `approach_detector_target_after_center_loss` decisions, then lost target
  evidence and fell back to memory-belief turns.

Follow-up:
- Add local target evidence trend features to the trace: depth delta, area
  delta, offset delta, and action outcome.
- Use those traces to build a short-horizon local visual-servo action scorer
  rather than adding more hand-authored one-step transitions.

  evidence as retained, lost, acquired, or remained absent.

  traces record raw detections while derived bbox area/center-offset features
  live in `policy_trace.json`.

- The latest official YOLO smoke showed that one-step servo rules are exhausted:
  the action-effect policy moved forward, then lost target evidence. A learned
  local action scorer needs reproducible self-supervised action-effect examples
  before it can replace hand-authored local-control rules.

  exist.

  exist.

  merged into examples.

  passed.

  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/local_action_effect_dataset_memory_evidence_frontier_action_effect_yolo_4ep_50steps_20260530_v1`.

Effect:
- The four-episode trace yielded `196` action-effect examples from `200` steps:
  `2` retained, `2` lost, `2` acquired, and `190` remained-absent transitions.
- The visible `tv_monitor` rows show a learnable failure signature: forward
  edge-tracking retained target evidence for two steps while bbox area dropped
  and absolute center offset increased, then the third forward step lost the
  target.
- This is data plumbing for a learned local controller, not a benchmark gain.

Follow-up:
- Train and evaluate an initial local action-effect scorer from these examples.
- Scale trace collection beyond the four-episode smoke before making any
  learned-controller claim.

## 2026-05-30 - Train Official Local Action-Effect Scorer

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_official_local_action_model.py`

- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Added a deterministic logistic local action-effect scorer trained from the
  official local action dataset.
- The model predicts `labels.next_target_visible` using pre-action local
  evidence plus candidate action identity.
- Added candidate-action scoring so later policy code can score
  `move_forward`, `turn_left`, and `turn_right` for the same evidence state.
- Added a CLI:
  `python -m objectnav_core.cli.train_habitat_official_local_action_model`.
- Changed the dataset exporter CLI to print a compact summary instead of the
  full example list while still writing complete JSON.

Reason:
- The action-effect dataset is useful only if it can drive learned action
  selection. This scorer is the first auditable model artifact that can replace
  local servo rules in a later official policy variant.

Verification:
- RED model tests failed because
  `objectnav_core.evaluation.habitat_official_local_action_model` did not
  exist.
- GREEN model tests proved candidate-action overrides rank a learned positive
  action above negative actions on synthetic examples.
- RED CLI test failed because
  `objectnav_core.cli.train_habitat_official_local_action_model` did not exist.
- RED compact-output test failed until the dataset exporter CLI stopped printing
  the full `examples` payload.
- Local focused model/dataset/packaging tests passed: `8` passed.
- Local focused official-memory/exporter/model set passed: `76` passed.
- Local `compileall` and `git diff --check` passed.
- Linux focused official-memory/exporter/model set in conda env `habitat`:
  `76` passed.
- Linux `compileall` and `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Linux export/train smokes wrote dataset/model artifacts under
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/`.

Effect:
- The YOLO action-effect model trained on `196` examples with `4` positive and
  `192` negative labels.
- Offline training metrics were: accuracy `0.989796`, log loss `0.057894`,
  Brier score `0.011688`, and mean prediction `0.026852`.
- These metrics are dominated by class imbalance and are not a benchmark claim.
- On the visible `tv_monitor` rows, the scorer ranked `turn_left` above
  repeated `move_forward`, including at the final visible step before target
  loss. This provides a learned hypothesis for the next policy variant.

Follow-up:
- Integrate the scorer as a separate official policy variant and compare
  against `memory_evidence_frontier`.
- Collect larger official traces before treating the scorer as a learned
  controller claim.

## 2026-05-30 - Integrate Official Learned Local Frontier Policy

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/run_habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py`

- `docs/experiments/2026-05-30-official-learned-local-frontier-yolo-query-smoke.md`
- `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-05-30-official-learned-local-frontier-policy.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Added the official policy variant `memory_learned_local_frontier`.
- Added preflight/manifest support for `local_action_model_path` and recorded
  local-action model metadata in `protocol_manifest.json`.
- Integrated the local action-effect scorer into detector-local control after
  failed detector-centering evidence exists.
- Added failed-turn candidate suppression so a learned scorer cannot choose a
  detector turn already observed to lose the target for the current bbox offset
  sign.
- Preserved `memory_evidence_frontier` as the hand-authored action-effect
  ablation.

Reason:
- The previous action-effect smoke removed the center/reacquire loop but still
  lost target evidence after forward edge-tracking. A learned local scorer is a
  cleaner ablation path than adding more fixed one-step heuristics.
- The first online learned-local smoke exposed a policy-boundary bug: the
  scorer could choose the very turn recorded as a failed centering action,
  recreating the old loop.

Verification:
- RED preflight/manifest/model-path tests failed before policy registration and
  CLI wiring.
- RED behavior test proved the synthetic scorer could choose a learned local
  action after failed centering.
- RED regression test failed because a learned scorer could repeat a known
  failed turn candidate.
- Local focused official-memory/exporter/model/evaluator gate: `80` passed.
- Local `compileall` returned cleanly.
- Local `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Linux focused official gate in conda env `habitat`: `80` passed.
- Linux `compileall` returned cleanly.
- Linux `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Initial Linux YOLO smoke wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/memory_learned_local_frontier_yolo_discovery_prior_local_action_model_trace_4ep_50steps_20260530_v1`.
- Fixed Linux YOLO smoke wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/memory_learned_local_frontier_suppressed_failed_turns_yolo_discovery_prior_local_action_model_trace_4ep_50steps_20260530_v1`.

Effect:
- Initial learned-local smoke was a regression: success `0/4`, SPL `0.0`,
  SoftSPL `0.0009902771347611306`, mean distance-to-goal
  `5.880594372749329`, with `22` `learned_local_action_score` decisions and
  `22` reacquisition decisions.
- After failed-turn candidate suppression, the learned-local policy recovered
  the action-effect baseline shape: success `0/4`, SPL `0.0`, SoftSPL
  `0.02518699682786324`, mean distance-to-goal `5.697803378105164`, with `4`
  learned-local decisions and `2` reacquisition decisions.
- This is still not a benchmark win. The learned local scorer currently
  restores the best diagnostic behavior but does not improve official success
  or SPL.

Follow-up:
- Keep `memory_learned_local_frontier` as a clean ablation boundary.
- Train on larger official traces with multi-frame evidence features before
  treating the learned scorer as a controller.
- Next serious policy should score short action sequences or use temporal
  trends in bbox offset, area, depth, and visibility.

- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Upgraded the official local action-effect dataset schema to
  `official-local-action-effect-v2`.
- Added `history_steps` and `horizon_steps` to the exporter API and CLI.
- Added past-only temporal features: previous target visibility, recent
  visible count, steps since last target visible, recent action/decision
  counts, detector confidence/area/depth/offset deltas, current depth, and
  suppressed-turn indicators.
- Added horizon labels: visible within horizon, visible at horizon, target lost
  within horizon, first loss delta, and best-future detector evidence deltas.
- Kept all future-derived values under `labels`, preserving the online policy
  boundary.

Reason:
- The learned-local smoke showed that a one-frame next-visibility scorer is too
  weak. The target can become closer by depth while bbox area drops and center
  offset grows before disappearing. The next controller needs temporal evidence
  features and larger official traces.

Verification:
- RED temporal exporter test failed because `history_steps`, `horizon_steps`,
  and temporal fields did not exist.
- RED CLI test failed because `--history-steps` and `--horizon-steps` were not
  accepted.
- Local dataset tests: `5` passed.
- Local focused official-memory/exporter/model/evaluator gate: `81` passed.
- Local `compileall` and `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Linux focused official gate in conda env `habitat`: `81` passed.
- Linux `compileall` and `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Linux four-episode temporal export wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/local_action_effect_dataset_temporal_learned_local_suppressed_yolo_4ep_50steps_20260530_v1`.
- Linux 20-episode discovery wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/discovery_yolo_world_valmini_20ep_80steps_20260530_v1`.
- Linux 20-episode query wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/memory_evidence_frontier_yolo_discovery_prior_detector_trace_20ep_80steps_20260530_v1`.
- Linux 20-episode temporal export wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/local_action_effect_dataset_temporal_memory_evidence_yolo_20ep_80steps_20260530_v1`.
- Linux temporal logistic smoke wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/local_action_effect_model_temporal_memory_evidence_yolo_20ep_80steps_20260530_v1/model.json`.

Effect:
- The 20-episode discovery pass produced `65` memory anchors from `1600`
  observations and `2676` YOLO detections.
- The 20-episode query still solved `0/20` official episodes, SPL `0.0`,
  SoftSPL `0.02945252626989075`; this is not benchmark progress.
- The v2 20-episode dataset has `1580` examples, `500` visible-before
  examples, `498` visible-after examples, `454` retained transitions, `46`
  lost transitions, and `44` acquired transitions.
- On the same dataset and `next_target_visible` label, temporal features
  improved offline logistic log loss from `0.174072` to `0.059308` and Brier
  score from `0.039334` to `0.011546`.

Follow-up:
- Carry these temporal features inside an online policy state so candidate
  scoring at runtime sees the same history as the dataset.
- Consider a horizon-loss or sequence-value label rather than only
  `next_target_visible`.
- Do not report the offline model as an ObjectNav result until an online policy
  improves official Habitat success/SPL.

## 2026-05-30 - Add Action-Conditioned Official Local Action Scorer Features

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_official_local_action_model.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_local_action_model.py`
- `docs/design/2026-05-30-official-action-conditioned-local-action-scorer.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-30-official-action-conditioned-local-action-scorer-yolo-smoke.md`
- `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-05-30-official-action-conditioned-local-action-scorer.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Added generated interaction features to the deterministic official local
  action model. Requested feature names of the form
  `left_feature__right_feature` are evaluated as products of finite base
  feature values.
- Candidate scoring now recomputes interaction values after candidate action
  overrides, so action-conditioned terms can change ranking for the same state.
- Added a unit test with a hand-authored model that prefers `move_forward` at
  low bbox offset and `turn_left` at high bbox offset.

Reason:
- The temporal v2 dataset improved offline calibration, but additive logistic
  state terms are identical across candidate actions. Interaction features are
  required before temporal/geometric state can affect action ranking.

Verification:
- RED test failed because interaction feature values were missing and the model
  still preferred `move_forward` at high offset.
- Local model tests: `4` passed.
- Local focused official-memory/exporter/model/evaluator gate: `82` passed.
- Local `compileall` and `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Linux focused official gate in conda env `habitat`: `82` passed.
- Linux `compileall` and `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Linux 20-episode interaction model training completed.

Effect:
- On the 20-episode v2 dataset, interaction temporal features improved offline
  log loss from additive temporal `0.059308` to `0.054151` and Brier score
  from `0.011546` to `0.010386`.
- The additive temporal model ranked `turn_right` first on all `500` visible
  rows; the interaction model produced varied best actions: `272`
  `turn_right`, `150` `turn_left`, and `78` `move_forward`.
- This is not an ObjectNav benchmark result. It proves candidate ranking can
  become state-dependent, but the on-policy data still has sparse
  counterfactual support.

Follow-up:
- Carry temporal features into the online learned-local policy so the
  interaction model is not forced to use training-set mean imputations.
- Inspect online traces before any larger official run.

  the loss and action priors. The visible-slice option lets us compare
  full-trace calibration against a local-control-specific model.

## 2026-05-30 - Run Temporal Learned-Local Model Comparison And YOLO Smoke

File:
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-30-official-temporal-learned-local-model-comparison-yolo-smoke.md`
- `docs/design/2026-05-30-official-local-action-candidate-score-report.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Synced the local action scoring/report slice to the Linux mirror.
- Trained eight temporal local-action logistic models crossing additive versus
  interaction features, `next_target_visible` versus
  `target_visible_at_horizon`, and full-trace versus current-visible-only
  training.
- Ran the candidate-score CLI for each model on all examples and the
  current-visible slice.
- Ran two four-episode official YOLO smokes with the temporal interaction
  models injected into `memory_learned_local_frontier`.

Reason:
- Offline calibration metrics alone do not prove a model changes candidate
  action rankings or online ObjectNav behavior. The comparison was needed to
  choose a credible smoke model and avoid overclaiming.

Verification:
- Linux focused official-memory/exporter/model/evaluator/packaging gate:
  `89` passed.
- Linux `compileall` and `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Offline comparison wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/local_action_model_comparison_temporal_yolo_20ep_80steps_20260530_v1`.
- Online YOLO smokes wrote detector and policy traces for both interaction
  models.

Effect:
- Common visible-slice calibration favored the interaction model trained on
  visible examples (`log_loss=0.074327`, `Brier=0.014401`).
- The full-trace interaction model had more diverse visible-slice rankings:
  `78` move-forward, `149` turn-left, and `273` turn-right best actions.
- Both online temporal interaction smokes remained official success `0/4`,
  SPL `0.0`, and SoftSPL `0.02518699682786324`.
- The learned-local branch fired only `3` to `4` times in `200` steps, so the
  local scorer is too narrow to move the current query policy by itself.

Follow-up:
- Treat the temporal local controller as a diagnostic component.
- Move to a broader active perception/search policy that seeks detector
  evidence, rather than only reacting after detector-centering failure.
- Add a detector-injecting official query CLI or extend the existing eval CLI
  so future YOLO smokes cannot accidentally run without a detector adapter.

## 2026-05-30 - Fix Rotation-Aware Active-Perception Frontier Scoring

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py`
- `docs/design/2026-05-30-official-active-perception-frontier-policy.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-30-official-active-perception-frontier-rotation-aware-yolo-smoke.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Added a regression test proving a valid side/standoff active-perception
  viewpoint keeps positive expected evidence.
- Removed the approach-heading cosine from active-perception view evidence.
  `bearing_rad` still drives navigation toward the selected frontier, but
  expected detector evidence is now scored as a prospective viewpoint where the
  agent can rotate in place before sensing the remembered anchor.
- Updated the active-perception design and added a detector-backed smoke
  experiment report.

Reason:
- The first active-perception smoke showed the branch was too brittle: the
  scorer could zero valid side viewpoints because it multiplied future sensing
  quality by the approach direction. That is the wrong geometry for a waypoint
  that can be reached and then scanned with in-place turns.

Verification:
- RED local regression failed before the fix with `view_bearing_quality=0.0`
  instead of `1.0`.
- GREEN local side-view regression passed after the fix.
- Local active-perception nearby tests: `3` passed.
- Local focused official-memory/exporter/model/evaluator/packaging gate:
  `94` passed.
- Local `compileall` and `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Linux focused official-memory/exporter/model/evaluator/packaging gate:
  `94` passed.
- Linux `compileall` and `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Linux detector-backed official YOLO smoke wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/memory_active_perception_frontier_rotation_aware_yolo_discovery_prior_detector_trace_4ep_50steps_20260530_v1`.

Effect:
- The rotation-aware smoke still produced official success `0/4` and SPL
  `0.0`, so this is not a benchmark improvement claim.
- SoftSPL improved diagnostically from `0.02518699682786324` to
  `0.03315001996589104`, and mean distance-to-goal moved from
  `5.697803378105164` to `5.637647747993469`.
- Active-perception decisions increased from `8` to `29`.
- Selected active-perception expected evidence was strictly positive
  (`0.4498654945233323..0.519892411627075`), and inspected top-candidate
  zero-evidence cases dropped from `21/40` to `0/145`.

Follow-up:
- Do not scale this as a paper result yet.
- Improve query memory-prior coverage and replace the lightweight standoff
  prior with a path-aware or learned evidence-acquisition value.
- Continue using only official Habitat `success`, `spl`, `soft_spl`, and
  `distance_to_goal` for benchmark-facing claims.

## 2026-05-30 - Add Path-Aware Active-Perception Viewpoint Scoring

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py`
- `docs/design/2026-05-30-official-path-aware-active-perception-viewpoints.md`
- `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-05-30-official-path-aware-active-perception-viewpoints.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-30-official-path-aware-active-perception-viewpoint-yolo-smoke.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Added active-perception candidates as reachable free `viewpoint_cell` plus
  adjacent unknown `frontier_cell` pairs.
- Added 4-connected BFS path distance through known free cells.
- Updated active-perception scoring to compute belief, standoff quality,
  bearing, and travel penalty from the free viewpoint instead of the unknown
  frontier cell.
- Added policy trace fields for `selected_viewpoint_cell` and
  `path_distance_m`.

Reason:
- The previous active-perception scorer was mechanically fixed but still
  treated unknown frontier cells as if they were sensing poses. A robot can
  only commit to reachable free viewpoints; this change makes the interface
  more compatible with real-robot navigation and later learned evidence
  acquisition.

Verification:
- RED free-viewpoint regression failed because `viewpoint_cell` was missing.
- RED path-distance regression failed because travel cost was not free-space
  path distance.
- Focused active-perception tests passed after implementation: `5` passed.
- Full official evaluator tests passed: `54` passed.
- Local focused official-memory/exporter/model/evaluator/packaging gate:
  `96` passed.
- Local `compileall` and `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Linux focused gate in conda env `habitat`: `96` passed.
- Linux `compileall` and `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Linux detector-backed YOLO smoke wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/memory_active_perception_frontier_path_aware_viewpoint_yolo_discovery_prior_detector_trace_4ep_50steps_20260530_v1`.

Effect:
- The smoke was a negative ablation, not a benchmark gain: official success
  remained `0/4`, SPL `0.0`, SoftSPL regressed from
  `0.03315001996589104` to `0.0009902771347611306`, and mean
  distance-to-goal increased to `6.065265536308289`.
- The trace now contains reachable viewpoint evidence and finite path
  distances; selected expected evidence stayed positive
  (`0.5103327473937014..0.5200065629595595`).
- Target-match detector calls dropped from `30` to `13`, suggesting cleaner
  waypoint scoring alone is not enough and may reduce opportunistic detector
  reacquisition.

Follow-up:
- Keep the reachable-viewpoint interface, but do not claim performance from it.
- Next algorithmic slice should add persistent active-perception commitment and
  explicit rotate/scan actions at or toward selected viewpoints.
- Use target-match recovery and official metrics to decide whether that scan
  phase is worth scaling.

Effect:
- The scan smoke remained negative: success `0/4`, SPL `0.0`, SoftSPL
  `0.02518699682786324`, mean distance-to-goal `5.697803378105164`.
- It recovered from the path-aware viewpoint smoke's SoftSPL
  `0.0009902771347611306`, but did not beat the simpler rotation-aware
  frontier-cell smoke SoftSPL `0.03315001996589104`.
- Target-match calls dropped to `4`, and the policy spent `20` steps orienting
  plus `20` steps scanning.

Follow-up:
- Do not scale the hand-authored scan phase as a benchmark candidate.
- Use the new trace fields to build a detector-view recall dataset and learn
  expected evidence or active-sensing value.
- Improve memory-prior coverage so more query episodes exercise memory rather
  than falling back with `no_matching_memory`.

- Fixed the view-recall dataset summary so
  `active_perception_example_count` includes explicit
  `active_perception` decision rows, not only newer phase/viewpoint fields.
- Re-exported view-recall datasets on the Linux mirror for active original,
  rotation-aware, path-aware, viewpoint-scan, memory-evidence, learned-local,
  and memory-belief policy traces.
- Added a hidden-to-visible diagnostic split to distinguish real detector
  reacquisition from rows where the target was already visible.

Reason:
- The first comparison showed some active-perception runs with active decisions
  but `active_perception_example_count=0`. That was a trace-format mismatch in
  the diagnostic metadata, not a policy behavior result.
- Raw future visibility is inflated by detector-continuity rows. A learned
  view-recall policy needs the harder hidden-to-visible examples.

Verification:
- RED regression test failed with `active_perception_example_count == 0` for a
  decision-only active-perception row.
- Local view-recall tests: `3` passed.
- Linux view-recall tests in conda env `habitat`: `3` passed.
- Re-exported ten comparison datasets with the corrected counter.

Effect:
- Corrected active counts for the active-perception variants:
  original `8`, rotation-aware `29`, path-aware `35`, viewpoint-scan `44`.
- Hidden-to-visible active positives were:
  original `8`, rotation-aware `9`, path-aware `8`, viewpoint-scan `4`.
- The viewpoint-scan phases still produced no positive labels:
  `orient_anchor=0/20`, `scan_anchor=0/20`.
- Across `116` active examples from the four active variants, all `29`
  hidden-to-visible active positives came from memory-frontier turn/move
  decisions, not from the hand-authored anchor scan phase.

Follow-up:
- Train the next view-recall baseline on hidden-to-visible labels, with
  detector-continuity rows down-weighted or stratified.
- Use the learned scorer to rank memory-frontier turn/viewpoint choices before
  running another official detector-backed smoke.

## 2026-05-30 - Train Official Hidden-to-Visible View-Recall Model

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_official_view_recall_model.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/train_habitat_official_view_recall_model.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/score_habitat_official_view_recall_model.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_view_recall_model.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/setup.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_ros_packaging.py`
- `docs/design/2026-05-30-official-view-recall-model.md`
- `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-05-30-official-view-recall-model.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-30-official-view-recall-model.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Added a deterministic logistic view-recall model for official
  `view_recall_dataset` artifacts.
- The default label is derived as
  `hidden_to_visible_within_horizon`, and default training/scoring filters to
  current-hidden rows so detector-continuity rows do not dominate.
- Added candidate-action scoring, score reports, grouped summaries, CSV export,
  train/score CLIs, and console scripts.

Reason:
- The view-recall dataset comparison showed that hand-authored scan rules are
  not where target recovery happens. The project needed a learnable offline
  value baseline before another official detector-backed smoke.

Verification:
- RED training test failed first because the model module did not exist.
- RED report tests failed for missing scoring/report functions.
- RED CLI/packaging tests failed for missing train/score modules and setup
  entries.
- Focused local view-recall model and packaging tests: `6` passed.
- Focused Linux view-recall model and packaging tests in conda env `habitat`:
  `6` passed.
- Linux scoring artifacts written under
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/view_recall_model_hidden_to_visible_20260530_v1`.

Effect:
- Memory-evidence 20-episode model trained on `1080` current-hidden examples
  with `53` positives and reached ROC AUC `0.975262`.
- That model transferred well to active original, rotation-aware, and
  path-aware traces, but degraded on viewpoint-scan (`0.828877` ROC AUC and
  only `1/10` positives in the top ten).
- Combined active training can suppress scan rows only after seeing scan
  negatives; when the scan variant is held out, the model over-scores unseen
  `orient_anchor` and `scan_anchor` rows despite their `0/20` positives.
- Candidate-action overrides are not policy-ready: memory-evidence training
  chooses all `turn_right`, while active-comparison training chooses all
  `turn_left`.

Follow-up:
- Build a candidate-view/action dataset with multiple candidate actions or
  viewpoints per state, including scan negatives.
- Train candidate ranking directly before integrating a learned view-recall
  scorer into the official online policy.
- Do not use the current observational candidate override as an official
  benchmark policy.

Change:
- Added a replay-based rollout dataset exporter for official active-perception
  candidate states.
- For each candidate row, the exporter creates a fresh env, replays the logged
  action prefix to the same decision state, branches the candidate action, and
  records detector-derived hidden-to-visible labels.
- Added JSON/CSV writing, CLI, and console script
  `objectnav_habitat_official_candidate_rollout_dataset`.
- Fixed the CLI `python -m` entrypoint guard and replay env `max_steps` budget
  after Linux smoke debugging.

Reason:
- The previous candidate dataset proved that existing traces expose candidate
  sets but do not contain counterfactual labels. Candidate learning needs real
  branch outcomes before a ranker can be trained honestly.

Verification:
- RED focused test failed because
  `habitat_official_candidate_rollout_dataset` did not exist.
- Local rollout dataset and packaging tests: `4` passed.
- RED CLI module guard regression failed because `python -m` produced empty
  stdout before the `__main__` guard.
- RED replay-budget regression failed because the fake env saw
  `max_steps=1` instead of the trace-prefix plus horizon budget.
- Local focused rollout/candidate/model/packaging gate: `13` passed.
- Local `compileall` for the new rollout module and CLI returned cleanly.
- Local `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Linux focused rollout/candidate/model/packaging gate in conda env `habitat`:
  `13` passed.
- Linux `compileall` and `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Linux active-original rollout smoke:
  `5` states, `15` candidate rollouts, `15` positives, `0` invalid.
- Linux active-scan rollout smoke:
  `10` states, `30` candidate rollouts, `30` positives, `0` invalid.

Effect:
- The local fake-env smoke verifies the same replay prefix is used for each
  branch and that candidate-specific detector outcomes produce different
  hidden-to-visible labels.
- Linux smokes verify the Habitat replay pipeline now produces valid branch
  rows without replay-ending invalids.
- The first real smokes were all-positive, so they validate infrastructure but
  are not yet suitable for discriminative candidate-ranker training.
- This is a diagnostic data-collection tool only. No online policy or official
  Habitat benchmark result changed.

Follow-up:
- Improve branch control or state sampling so the rollout export yields real
  negative candidate labels.
- Train a candidate-value model only on rollout rows with real labels.

Change:
- Ran full active-trace action-matrix exports on Linux for original,
  rotation-aware, path-aware, and viewpoint-scan active-perception traces.
- Compared horizon-5 recovery labels against horizon-1 immediate first-action
  controls for `turn_left`, `turn_right`, and `move_forward`.
- Updated the design and experiment notes to distinguish short-horizon recovery
  labels from immediate first-action labels.

Reason:
- The small action-matrix smoke broke the all-positive candidate-rollout
  pattern, but the diagnostic controller scans left after the forced first
  action. A horizon-1 control was needed before training or claiming a
  counterfactual first-action scorer.

Verification:
- Linux horizon-5 all-active action matrix completed with `399` rollouts,
  `172` hidden-to-visible positives, and `0` invalid rows.
- Linux horizon-5 current-hidden subset had `172/309` positives:
  `move_forward 53/103`, `turn_left 96/103`, `turn_right 23/103`.
- Linux horizon-1 all-active action matrix completed with `399` rollouts,
  `8` positives, and `0` invalid rows.
- Linux horizon-1 current-hidden subset had `8/309` positives:
  `move_forward 0/103`, `turn_left 7/103`, `turn_right 1/103`.

Effect:
- The rollout machinery now has a clean counterfactual recovery dataset, but
  the current horizon-5 labels are dominated by delayed scan recovery.
- The immediate first-action signal is too sparse for a useful action scorer.
- No online policy, official Habitat metric, or benchmark-facing claim changed.

Follow-up:
- Redesign the branch follow-up controller before training a learned action
  scorer.
- Keep immediate action effect, short-horizon recovery, and exact viewpoint
  utility as separate label types.

## 2026-05-31 - Add Repeat-First Action-Matrix Follow-Up

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_official_candidate_rollout_dataset.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/export_habitat_official_candidate_rollout_dataset.py`

- `docs/experiments/2026-05-30-official-candidate-rollout-labeling.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Added configurable branch follow-up policy metadata and CLI plumbing for the
  rollout exporter.
- Preserved the existing `left_scan` default for reproducibility.
- Added `repeat_first_action`, which repeats the explicit action branch across
  the rollout horizon so action-matrix rows represent symmetric macro-action
  interventions.

Reason:
- The horizon-5 action matrix had real negatives but was confounded by the
  follow-up controller, which turned left after the first branch action.
  Repeat-first labels are a cleaner bridge toward learned active retrieval.

Verification:
- RED local tests failed first because `branch_followup_policy` was missing
  from the API and CLI.
- Local rollout tests after implementation: `6` passed.
- Local focused rollout/candidate/model/packaging gate: `15` passed.
- Local compileall for the rollout module and CLI returned cleanly.
- Local `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Linux focused rollout tests in conda env `habitat`: `6` passed.
- Linux repeat-first full active-trace exports completed with `399` rollouts,
  `201` positives, and `0` invalid rows.

Effect:
- Current-hidden repeat-first recovery by action:
  `move_forward 49/103`, `turn_left 96/103`, `turn_right 56/103`.
- Repeat-first removes the hidden left-scan continuation and recovers many more
  right-turn branches than `left_scan` (`56/103` versus `23/103`).
- Binary recovery is still not enough for a learned scorer because `turn_left`
  remains near-oracle. Time-to-visible is more promising: strict fastest actions
  among current-hidden states were `turn_left 29`, `turn_right 11`, and
  `move_forward 1`, with `62` ties.

Follow-up:
- Build a cost-aware action utility dataset/report from repeat-first rollouts.
- Use held-out trace families before considering online policy integration.

## 2026-05-31 - Train First Cost-Aware Action Utility Baseline

- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_official_candidate_rollout_action_utility_model.py`

- `docs/experiments/2026-05-30-official-candidate-rollout-labeling.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Added a deterministic linear action-utility model trained from action-matrix
  report states.
- Labels are `1 / time_to_visible_steps` for recovering actions and `0` for
  non-recovering actions.
- Added scoring and leave-one-source evaluation.
- Added action-decision and action-step interaction features after a RED test
  showed the model could not represent early/late action switches.

Reason:
- Binary repeat-first recovery remained too easy for an always-left baseline.
  A cost-aware scorer is the right next probe before any online policy work.

Verification:
- RED tests failed first for missing model module, CLI, setup entry, and then
  missing action-step interaction capacity.
- Local utility model tests: `4` passed after the first implementation and `4`
  passed again after action-step interactions.
- Local focused utility/rollout/report/candidate/model/packaging gate:
  `21` passed.
- Local compileall and `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Linux utility/packaging tests in conda env `habitat`: `5` passed.
- Linux training wrote artifacts under
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/action_utility_model_repeat_first_all_active_step_interaction_yolo_20260531_v1`.

Effect:
- Full-report score: `91/103` chosen actions are fastest, `96/103` recover,
  mean utility regret `0.038835`.
- This matches the always-`turn_left` baseline on the same report, even though
  the learned model chooses `turn_left 71`, `move_forward 25`, and
  `turn_right 7`.
- Leave-one-source evaluation is worse than always-left:
  `84/103` fastest, `89/103` success, mean regret `0.052427`.
- The model is useful as a diagnostic negative baseline, not an online policy.

Follow-up:
- Add richer pre-decision state features to the action report: memory-anchor
  bearing, relative heading, local depth/occupancy clearance, and recent
  detector evidence.
- Only revisit online integration after a learned model beats simple global
  action baselines on held-out trace families.
