  features suitable for another held-out utility-model attempt.
- No Habitat datasets were regenerated and no new benchmark/model improvement is
  claimed from this local plumbing step.

Follow-up:
- Regenerate repeat-first action-matrix datasets/reports so real artifacts
  contain `state_features`.
- Retrain/evaluate against always-left and the previous weak model with
  leave-one-source-family validation.

## 2026-05-31 - Regenerate Feature-Bearing Action Matrix Artifacts

File:
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-30-official-candidate-rollout-labeling.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Regenerated the four repeat-first action-matrix rollout datasets on Linux
  after adding `state_features`.
- Built a new aggregate current-hidden action-matrix report from those datasets.
- Trained default and tuned feature-aware linear utility models.
- Wrote a baseline comparison artifact for always-action baselines, the old
  step-interaction model, and the new feature-aware models.
- Ran scratch hyperparameter/subset/pairwise-ranking probes to test whether the
  new features generalize beyond source-specific overfit.

Reason:
- The feature schema only matters if real Habitat rollouts carry it through to
  the report and a held-out model comparison. The previous report was stale and
  could not test whether geometry/evidence features help.

Verification:
- Real one-state smoke export produced `3` rollouts with non-empty
  `state_features`.
- Full regenerated rollout counts matched the prior repeat-first counts:
  original `75`, rotation `87`, path `105`, scan `132`; aggregate `399`
  rollouts, `201` positives, `0` invalid.
- New aggregate report:
  `103` current-hidden states and `309` action rollouts.
- Report feature audit found `25` feature keys; core geometry/depth/memory
  fields were present on all `103` states.

Effect:
- Tuned feature-aware linear model improved full-report metrics to `95/103`
  fastest, `100/103` success, regret `0.018608`.
- It did **not** beat always-`turn_left` under leave-one-source validation:
  both score `91/103` fastest, `96/103` success, regret `0.038835`.
- The default feature model with the old training hyperparameters collapsed to
  always-`move_forward`.
- A scratch pairwise-ranking probe reached `99/103` fastest on the full report
  but only `85/103` leave-one-source, also not policy-ready.

Follow-up:
- Do not integrate this model online.
- Mine hard states where always-left is not fastest and collect more diverse
  counterfactual rollouts before adding another policy model.
- Consider exact candidate-viewpoint/state-restore labels or a richer action
  space; current four-trace repeat-first supervision is too source-skewed for a
  publishable learned controller claim.

Change:
- Added an offline hard-state miner for official candidate-rollout
  action-matrix reports.
- The default slice keeps states where the baseline action, currently
  always-`turn_left`, is absent from `fastest_actions`.
- Added optional `--include-baseline-ties` diagnostics for states where
  always-left is co-fastest but not strict fastest.

  module guard, and console script registration.

Reason:

  source-family validation. The next useful step is to expose the truly hard
  states and source/category skew before collecting or relabeling more data.

Verification:
- RED tests failed first for the missing miner module, CLI module, and console
  script registration.
- Local miner and packaging tests: `5 passed`.
- Linux miner and packaging tests in conda env `habitat`: `5 passed`.

  `12/103` states, strict fastest counts `turn_right 11` and
  `move_forward 1`.
- Linux hard-or-tie diagnostic artifact generated:
  `74/103` states, consisting of `12` not-fastest states and `62` baseline-tie
  states.

Effect:
- The current true always-left-failure slice is very small and all `tv_monitor`:
  source split original `1`, rotation-aware `5`, path-aware `5`, and
  viewpoint-scan `1`.
- This strengthens the negative conclusion: the current four-trace
  repeat-first supervision is not balanced enough for another policy-model
  claim.

Follow-up:
- Collect broader hard-state rollouts with category/source diversity before
  retraining utility models.
- Treat baseline-tie states as ambiguity diagnostics unless a future objective
  explicitly learns tie-breaking or risk-sensitive action choice.

  hidden-to-visible candidates, and `0` invalid candidate restores.

  candidate, with current-view visibility preserved separately from
  hidden-to-visible candidate viewpoint recovery.
- Candidate grid assumptions are recorded in each row as grid size, cell size,
  origin, and candidate episode-relative `x/z`.
- On the bounded phase/path sample, at least one candidate viewpoint was visible
  for `16/24` states and hidden-to-visible for `15/24` states, compared with
  only `1/24` current-view state-restore positives.

  policy claim.

  labels can compare uniform scan visibility with anchor-facing visibility.

  source artifact. A paper-facing learned memory component needs source-level
  validation before it can be considered for online policy integration.

Verification:
- RED tests failed first for missing
  `evaluate_candidate_viewpoint_ranker_leave_one_source`,
  `--leave-one-source-output`, and multi-dataset CLI parsing.
- Focused local ranker tests after implementation: `6 passed`.
- Full local `src/objectnav_core/tests` suite: `435 passed`.
- Local `compileall`, `git diff --check`, and touched-file whitespace scan
  passed before Linux sync.
- Linux focused ranker tests in conda env `habitat`: `6 passed`.
- Linux targeted compileall for ranker module, CLI, and tests passed.
- Linux source-diverse candidate-viewpoint exports completed.
- Linux source-diverse ranker scoring completed:
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/candidate_viewpoint_ranker_source_diverse_yolo_20260531_v1`.

Effect:
- Added source artifacts:
  `candidate_viewpoint_restore_active_frontier_4ep_yolo_20260531_v1`,
  `candidate_viewpoint_restore_rotation_aware_4ep_yolo_20260531_v1`,
  `candidate_viewpoint_restore_path_aware_4ep_yolo_20260531_v1`, and
  `candidate_viewpoint_restore_viewpoint_scan_4ep_yolo_20260531_v1`.
- Active-frontier and rotation-aware 4-episode artifacts each had `10/10`
  invalid candidate restores, so they contributed no label-available rows.
- Path-aware and viewpoint-scan artifacts each added `2` states and `10`
  candidates, with `7` and `6` hidden-to-visible candidate rows respectively.
- Combined source-diverse ranker training/eval used `135` current-hidden,
  label-available candidates from `27` states.
- Combined train-set score report: oracle `19/27`, model `18/27`, top-rank
  `14/27`, top-score `14/27`.
- Leave-one-source aggregate over label-available sources: oracle `19/27`,
  model `17/27`, top-rank `14/27`, top-score `14/27`.
- On the largest 20-episode holdout alone, the model tied top-rank/top-score:
  `13/23` recovered with oracle `15/23`.

Follow-up:
- Generate larger independent source artifacts; current source-diverse gain is
  real but partly from two-state auxiliary holdouts.
- Keep the ranker offline until source-held-out recovery improves on large
  independent traces, not just small auxiliary traces.
- Investigate why some 4-episode sources produce invalid candidate restores
  before relying on them for training data.

## 2026-05-31 - Fix Frontier-Cell Candidate-Viewpoint Restore Compatibility

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_official_candidate_rollout_dataset.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_candidate_rollout_dataset.py`

- `docs/experiments/2026-05-31-official-candidate-viewpoint-ranker.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Added a candidate-viewpoint restore fallback from `viewpoint_cell` to
  `frontier_cell`.
- Added a regression test for source traces whose candidates only contain
  `frontier_cell`.
- Regenerated the active-frontier and rotation-aware 4-episode
  candidate-viewpoint restore datasets as v2 artifacts.
- Re-ran the source-diverse candidate-viewpoint ranker with the repaired v2
  source artifacts.

Reason:
- Systematic debugging showed the invalid restores were not Habitat-Sim pose
  failures. The exporter rejected older frontier-style candidate rows before
  restore because they lacked `viewpoint_cell`, even though their
  `frontier_cell` is the best available candidate pose for that policy family.

Verification:
- RED regression failed first with `candidate_x_m is None` for a
  frontier-only candidate.
- Focused local regression passed after the fallback.
- Local dataset/ranker focused gate: `29 passed`.
- Full local `src/objectnav_core/tests` suite: `436 passed`.
- Linux focused regression passed: `1 passed`.
- Linux targeted candidate-viewpoint/ranker tests: `8 passed`.
- Regenerated Linux active-frontier v2 artifact:
  `2` states, `10` candidates, `10` hidden-to-visible, `0` invalid.
- Regenerated Linux rotation-aware v2 artifact:
  `2` states, `10` candidates, `10` hidden-to-visible, `0` invalid.
- Re-scored source-diverse ranker v2:
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/candidate_viewpoint_ranker_source_diverse_yolo_20260531_v2`.

Effect:
- Source-diverse candidate rows increased from `135` to `155` current-hidden,
  label-available candidates.
- Positive candidate rows increased from `82` to `102`.
- Leave-one-source recovery improved from model `17/27` vs baselines `14/27`
  to model `22/31` vs baselines `18/31`, with oracle `23/31`.
- Equivalently, among oracle-recoverable states, leave-one-source recovery is
  `22/23` for the model and `18/23` for the baselines.
- The largest 20-episode holdout improved from a model/baseline tie to model
  `14/23` vs top-rank/top-score `13/23`, oracle `15/23`; among
  oracle-recoverable states this is `14/15` vs `13/15`.

Follow-up:
- Keep the result framed as offline candidate-viewpoint supervision.
- Next useful gate is an online active-perception policy slice that uses the
  ranker to choose candidate viewpoints, still without teleportation or
  route-follower shortcuts.

Change:

  ObjectNav evaluator.

  candidates that match the restore-label training distribution.

Reason:
- Offline source-held-out candidate-viewpoint ranking improved over top-rank and
  top-score baselines, but that evidence was not yet an online policy result.
  This slice tests whether the learned selector transfers into official Habitat
  action execution without teleportation.

Verification:
- RED tests failed first for missing model loader, missing CLI flag, missing
  selector kwarg, and missing episode-loop model plumbing.

  hand-score rank `5` with `top_k=2`; this reproduced the online extrapolation
  bug.

- Local compileall and `git diff --check`: clean.
- Linux focused official/ranker tests in conda env `habitat`: `74 passed`.
- Linux compileall and `git diff --check`: clean.
- Linux preflight with the real source-diverse v2 ranker model succeeded and
  recorded a 49-feature logistic model in the protocol manifest.

Effect:
- The ranker is now mechanically available for online policy ablations, but the
  first YOLO smoke is negative.

  ranks included `40`, `53`, and `84`.
- Fixed v2 smoke:
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/ranker_guided_active_perception_yolo_4ep_50steps_20260531_v2`.
- v2 official metrics: success `0/4`, SPL `0.0`, SoftSPL
  `0.0009902771347611306`.
- v2 detector trace: `0` target-match calls and `0` target-match detections.
- Comparable hand-score active-perception smokes on the same memory prior had
  nonzero target-match calls: `39` for the original active-perception smoke and
  `30` for the rotation-aware smoke.

Follow-up:
- Do not claim an online ranker improvement from this result.
- Keep the ranker path as an ablation hook.
- Next useful algorithmic step is online rollout/value supervision or a

  ranker as the default policy.

## 2026-05-31 - Run Candidate-Rank Ablation for Online Ranker Transfer

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_official_candidate_viewpoint_ranker_model.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/train_habitat_official_candidate_viewpoint_ranker.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_candidate_viewpoint_ranker_model.py`
- `docs/design/2026-05-31-official-ranker-guided-active-perception.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-31-official-ranker-guided-active-perception-candidate-rank-ablation.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Added an auditable `--exclude-feature` option to the candidate-viewpoint
  ranker trainer.
- Recorded excluded feature names in the model artifact training filter.
- Trained a source-diverse no-`candidate_rank` ranker and ran the matched
  ranker-guided active-perception YOLO smoke.

Reason:
- The previous online smoke suggested the logistic ranker might be overusing
  `candidate_rank`: before the top-K guard it extrapolated to high hand-score
  ranks, and after the guard it selected hand-score rank `4` on most active
  online steps. The ablation tests whether removing that single feature is
  enough to recover useful online behavior.

Verification:
- RED tests failed first for missing `excluded_feature_names` API support and
  missing `--exclude-feature` CLI parsing.
- Focused local ranker tests after implementation: `8 passed`.
- Linux focused ranker tests in conda env `habitat`: `8 passed`.
- Linux preflight with the no-`candidate_rank` model succeeded and recorded a
  48-feature logistic ranker in the protocol manifest.
- Linux offline no-`candidate_rank` ranker artifact:
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/candidate_viewpoint_ranker_source_diverse_no_candidate_rank_yolo_20260531_v1`.
- Linux online smoke artifact:
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/ranker_guided_active_perception_no_candidate_rank_yolo_4ep_50steps_20260531_v1`.

Effect:
- Offline source-diverse recovery stayed unchanged: model `22/31` all
  current-hidden states and `22/23` oracle-recoverable states, versus
  top-rank/top-score `18/31` and `18/23`.
- Offline selected-rank distribution became less concentrated on rank `4`
  (`12/31` states instead of `26/31`).
- Online behavior did not improve: success `0/4`, SPL `0.0`, SoftSPL
  `0.0009902771347611306`, and `0` target-match detector calls.
- Online trace still selected hand-score rank `4` on `48/49` ranker-active
  steps, so `candidate_rank` is not the root cause by itself.

Follow-up:
- Treat the offline ranker as a diagnostic tool, not a deployable policy.
- Train the next selector on online downstream utility: detector evidence gain,
  official progress gain, or a short-horizon rollout success proxy.
- Keep source/state grouping in any new learning target to avoid leakage.

## 2026-05-31 - Diagnose Active-Perception Controller Mismatch

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py`
- `docs/design/2026-05-30-official-active-perception-viewpoint-scan.md`
- `docs/design/2026-05-31-official-ranker-guided-active-perception.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-31-official-active-perception-controller-mismatch.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-31-official-ranker-guided-active-perception-candidate-rank-ablation.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Added short-lived active-perception viewpoint commitment so an in-progress
  option keeps pursuing the chosen `viewpoint_cell` instead of reselecting every
  step.
- Added a bounded blocked-target local scan before abandoning an aligned
  active-perception target whose center-depth corridor is blocked.
- Ran the matched no-ranker hand-score smoke under the same changed controller
  as the no-`candidate_rank` learned-ranker smoke.
- Documented the controller mismatch and the learned-vs-hand-score comparison.

Reason:
- Counterfactual branch diagnostics showed the top-K candidate branches were
  often recoverable under the exporter continuation, while the online policy
  still failed to realize detector evidence. The likely mismatch was no longer
  just candidate ranking, but the controller that executes a selected
  viewpoint.

Verification:
- RED regression tests failed first for viewpoint commitment and blocked-target
  scan behavior, then passed after implementation.
- Linux no-rank ranker with sticky commitment only:
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/ranker_guided_active_perception_no_candidate_rank_sticky_yolo_4ep_50steps_20260531_v1`.
- Linux no-rank ranker with sticky plus blocked scan:
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/ranker_guided_active_perception_no_candidate_rank_sticky_blocked_scan_yolo_4ep_50steps_20260531_v1`.
- Linux matched hand-score with sticky plus blocked scan:
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/memory_active_perception_frontier_sticky_blocked_scan_yolo_4ep_50steps_20260531_v1`.
- Sticky-only no-rank smoke remained negative: success `0/4`, SPL `0.0`,
  SoftSPL `0.0009902771347611306`, and `0` target-match calls.
- Sticky plus blocked-scan no-rank smoke recovered partial detector evidence:
  success `0/4`, SPL `0.0`, SoftSPL `0.02518699682786324`, and `4`
  target-match calls.
- Matched hand-score sticky plus blocked-scan smoke produced the same official
  metrics and target evidence: success `0/4`, SPL `0.0`, SoftSPL
  `0.02518699682786324`, and `4` target-match calls.

Effect:
- The controller change matters because it restores some target evidence that
  the earlier online learned-ranker smoke failed to realize.
- The current learned ranker still has no demonstrated online advantage over
  the hand-score active-perception selector.
- This remains a negative benchmark result: official success is still `0/4` and
  SPL is still `0.0`.

Follow-up:
- Keep the controller state-machine tests as guardrails.
- Train the next candidate selector on online option value under the same
  sticky plus blocked-scan continuation, not on teleport/restore visibility
  alone.
- Preserve source/restored-state grouping for all new supervision splits.

Change:
- Added a candidate option-value exporter that replays an official Habitat
  policy trace to a candidate-bearing state, expands top-K active-perception
  viewpoints, forces one candidate option at a time under the sticky plus
  blocked-scan continuation, and labels whether the target becomes visible
  within the option rollout.
- Added JSON and CSV output for the option-value dataset.
- Added a CLI wrapper for YOLO/Grounding-DINO/no-detector export.
- Threaded custom `--label` names through ranker state-fold evaluation so
  option-value fold diagnostics do not fall back to the old
  candidate-viewpoint restore label.
- Ran a tiny same-trace option-value ranker and a matched online diagnostic.

Reason:
- The offline candidate-viewpoint restore labels were useful for supervision
  but too optimistic for direct online transfer. The next target needed to
  represent actual downstream option utility under the same controller used by
  the online policy.

Verification:
- Local focused option/ranker gate after the custom-label fix: `37 passed`.
- Local `compileall` exit `0`.
- CLI help for the option-value exporter completed.
- Local `git diff --check`: clean.
- Touched-file whitespace scan: clean.
- Linux focused option/ranker gate after the custom-label fix: `37 passed`.
- Linux `compileall` exit `0`.
- Linux option-value export artifact:
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/candidate_option_value_sticky_blocked_scan_yolo_4ep_20260531_v1`.
- Linux online diagnostic artifact:
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/option_value_ranker_guided_active_perception_yolo_4ep_50steps_20260531_v1`.

Effect:
- The option-value dataset produced `8` states, `40` candidate options, `0`
  invalid options, and `16` hidden-to-visible positives.
- All `8/8` selected states were oracle-recoverable under option rollouts.
- Hand-score top-rank and top-score recovered `0/8` states; positives were
  consistently at candidate ranks `2` and `3`.
- The tiny option-value ranker recovered `8/8` states in same-trace state-fold
  diagnostics versus top-rank/top-score `0/8`. This is only a plumbing and
  learnability check, not generalization evidence.
- The matched online option-value ranker smoke did not improve over the
  fixed-controller hand-score selector: success `0/4`, SPL `0.0`, SoftSPL
  `0.02518699682786324`, and `4` target-match calls for both.
- The option-value model did change online choices, selecting hand-score rank
  `3` on `40/44` ranker-active steps, but this did not change official metrics.

Follow-up:
- Treat option-value labels as a promising supervision substrate, not an online
  policy win yet.
- Expand option-value export across more sources and enforce source/state
  separation before relying on learned selector evidence.
- Add richer utility labels for detector evidence gain, official progress gain,
  or stop-success eligibility within the option rollout.
- Inspect local continuation after selecting rank `2/3` candidates because the
  remaining failure is likely downstream of first candidate selection.

- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_official_candidate_rollout_dataset.py`

- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Extended candidate option-value rows with detector confidence gain fields.
- Added official `distance_to_goal` progress fields and labels.
- Added a supervision-only STOP probe after each valid option rollout and
  records official success/SPL/SoftSPL/distance from that branch.
- Added `--option-progress-threshold-m` to the option-value exporter CLI.
- Added ranker regression coverage to keep option outcome fields out of online
  feature extraction.

Reason:
- The binary hidden-to-visible label is not close enough to official ObjectNav
  utility. A memory candidate can reveal the target and still fail to improve
  official progress or support a valid STOP. These labels move the offline
  target closer to benchmark-relevant behavior without leaking privileged
  metrics online.

Verification:
- RED tests failed first for missing detector gain fields, missing official
  progress fields, and missing CLI threshold parsing.
- Local candidate-rollout tests: `30 passed`.
- Local ranker tests: `10 passed`.
- Local combined focused gate: `40 passed`.
- Local `compileall`: exit `0`.
- Local CLI help showed `--option-progress-threshold-m`.
- Linux focused gate in conda env `habitat`: `40 passed`.
- Linux `compileall`: exit `0`.
- Linux no-detector Habitat smoke:
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/candidate_option_utility_sticky_blocked_scan_nodetector_1state_20260531_v1`.

Effect:
- The no-detector Habitat smoke exported `1` state, `2` candidate options, and
  `0` invalid options.
- Both rows populated official distance fields through `env.get_metrics()`.
- Both rows had `0.0` best distance delta and STOP-probe success `0.0`, so the
  smoke is a schema/boundary check rather than a policy result.
- The ranker feature allowlist still excludes outcome fields such as
  `distance_to_goal_delta_m`, `best_distance_to_goal_delta_m`, and
  `stop_probe_success`.

Follow-up:
- Re-export the broader option-value dataset with YOLO enabled and compare
  detector, official-progress, and STOP-success labels.
- Train selectors on the richer labels only after broader source/state-separated
  support exists.
- Keep the labels documented as offline privileged supervision only.

Change:
- Added `memory_active_perception_frontier_pathfinder_suffix`, an oracle
  diagnostic policy that keeps the memory-active-perception exploration stack
  and switches to a Habitat pathfinder suffix after a target detector match.
- Added an injectable pathfinder suffix controller for local tests and a lazy
  Habitat `ShortestPathFollower` adapter for Linux runs.
- Added CLI flag `--pathfinder-suffix-goal-radius-m`.
- Recorded suffix debug in per-episode policy debug and policy traces.

Reason:
- Previous online smokes produced target matches but still had `0/4` success.
  The diagnostic isolates whether the remaining bottleneck is terminal approach
  and STOP rather than exploration/memory reacquisition.

Verification:
- Local RED/GREEN tests covered policy registration, suffix activation, action
  mapping, episode goal extraction, missing-goal fallback, and CLI forwarding.
- Local focused eval/CLI gate: `76 passed`.
- Local `compileall`: exit `0`.
- Local CLI help showed `--pathfinder-suffix-goal-radius-m`.
- Local `git diff --check`: clean.
- Linux focused eval/CLI gate in conda env `habitat`: `76 passed`.

- Linux radius-`1.0` YOLO smoke:
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/pathfinder_suffix_active_perception_yolo_4ep_50steps_20260531_v1`.
- Linux radius-`0.05` YOLO smoke:
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/pathfinder_suffix_active_perception_yolo_4ep_100steps_radius005_20260531_v1`.

Effect:
- The `1.0` m suffix-radius run activated on the tv-monitor episode and reached
  `0.7462759613990784` m final distance, but official success stayed `0/4`
  because the ObjectNav config uses `success_distance: 0.1`.
- The `0.05` m suffix-radius run produced the first nonzero official success in
  this sequence: success `1/4`, SPL `0.24261777449152924`, SoftSPL
  `0.24163039972246855`.
- The successful episode was `tv_monitor`, activated the suffix at step `4`,
  stopped at step `47`, and ended at distance `0.061577994376420975`.
- This is oracle-only diagnostic evidence, not a benchmark-valid policy result,
  because the suffix uses Habitat episode goal/viewpoint positions after
  detection.

Follow-up:
- Replace the oracle suffix with detector-depth target projection and a
  Habitat-compatible PointNav/VLFM-style local navigation suffix.
- Improve memory/source coverage and target reacquisition so more than one of
  four episodes reaches suffix activation.
- Keep all future comparisons separated into oracle diagnostic versus
  benchmark-valid policy results.

## 2026-05-31 - Diagnose TargetNav DDPPO Contract Mismatch

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py`
- `docs/design/2026-05-31-official-targetnav-ddppo-backend.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-31-official-targetnav-ddppo-contract-diagnostic.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Added robust TargetNav belief smoothing metadata in code before the
  diagnostic: detector-depth projections now carry measurement variance,
  max-range / edge-touching measurements are downweighted, and the old EMA path
  remains available when no uncertainty metadata exists.
- Ran a diagnostic oracle-goal DDPPO fork on the `tv_monitor` episode to test
  whether failure remains when the detector target belief is removed.
- Ran a second oracle contract ablation that changed ObjectNav turn/sensor
  settings to match the HM3D depth DDPPO checkpoint training contract.

Reason:
- The four-episode DDPPO smoke still had official success `0/4`. The
  investigation needed to separate detector-belief noise from PointGoal,
  action, depth, and environment-contract errors before adding another planner
  rule.

Verification:
- Local focused eval/backend gate after robust belief smoothing: `99 passed`.
- Linux focused eval/backend gate after robust belief smoothing: `99 passed`.
- Linux `compileall` after robust belief smoothing: clean.
- Linux four-episode robust-belief DDPPO smoke wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/targetnav_ddppo_robust_belief_yolo_4ep_100steps_20260531_v1`
  and produced success `0/4`, SPL `0.0`, SoftSPL
  `0.07662481056928958`, and mean distance `5.309223055839539`.
- Linux oracle DDPPO diagnostic wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/oracle_targetnav_ddppo_tv_monitor_20260531_v1`.
- Linux oracle contract ablation wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/oracle_targetnav_ddppo_contract_ablation_tv_monitor_20260531_v1`.

Effect:
- Robust smoothing mildly improved the DDPPO YOLO smoke, but did not solve
  ObjectNav: success remained `0/4`.
- Oracle goal with the default ObjectNav contract still failed on `tv_monitor`;
  final distance was `4.759258270263672`, and DDPPO became physically stuck
  while repeatedly issuing `move_forward`.
- The in-repo TargetNav PointGoal adapter matched Habitat's native
  `PointGoalWithGPSCompassSensor` math to within about `5e-7`, so the adapter
  sign convention is not the current blocker.
- Changing only ObjectNav `turn_angle` from `30` to the checkpoint's trained
  `10` degrees improved final distance to `1.9689984321594238`.
- Matching the checkpoint's broader training contract (`turn_angle=10`, 256x256
  RGB-D, 90 degree HFOV, camera height `1.25`, depth `0.0-10.0`, sliding
  enabled) made the oracle diagnostic succeed: final distance
  `0.029823284596204758`, success `1.0`, SPL `0.9851193591581345`, SoftSPL
  `0.9812302867951809`.

Follow-up:
- Treat the downloaded HM3D PointNav checkpoint as a contract-sensitive
  diagnostic backend, not as a solved benchmark-valid ObjectNav local policy.
- Train or fine-tune a PointNav/TargetNav local policy under the official
  ObjectNav sensor/action contract, or choose a local planner whose contract
  already matches the benchmark loop.
- Keep oracle-goal and DDPPO-matched-contract runs clearly labeled as
  diagnostic-only evidence.

Change:
- Confirmed the existing `memory_active_perception_frontier_targetnav_fmm`
  backend is implemented and locally/Linux unit-tested.

  `targetnav_fmm_active_perception_yolo_4ep_100steps_20260531_v1` and
  `targetnav_fmm_no-false-stop_yolo_4ep_100steps_20260531_v1`.
- Fixed a false-stop FMM failure: when sparse map coverage made the current
  cell the only reachable free cell, FMM now falls back instead of stopping
  meters from the target.
- Added an offline oracle TargetNav local-policy dataset exporter with JSON/CSV
  output, explicit `oracle_teacher_only` provenance, fake-env tests, CLI, and
  packaging entry point.

Reason:
- FMM over an online occupancy/cost map is the standard SemExp-style classical
  substrate and is faster to validate than training. The project needed a real
  official-metric answer for whether the existing FMM path already solves the
  local-navigation problem.
- The DDPPO diagnostic showed that off-the-shelf PointNav is contract-mismatched;
  the dataset exporter is the first step toward training a local policy under
  the official ObjectNav action/sensor contract.

  `4 passed`.
- Linux FMM focused tests in conda env `habitat` passed:
  `4 passed`.
- Local TargetNav local-policy dataset and packaging tests passed:
  `6 passed`.

  `0.0009902771347611306`, mean distance `5.880594372749329`, detector
  target-match calls `2`.
- Post-fix FMM smoke: success `0/4`, SPL `0.0`, SoftSPL
  `0.01716268349268174`, mean distance `5.758422017097473`, detector
  target-match calls `77`.

Effect:
- FMM is now a verified benchmark-valid classical baseline path, not just an
  untested design.
- The premature FMM stop condition is covered by a regression test.
- FMM still does not solve the four-episode ObjectNav smoke; the remaining
  failure is sparse-map/oscillatory local control after target reacquisition,
  plus weak target-match coverage on non-`tv_monitor` episodes.
- The new exporter creates a clear bridge toward learned local navigation under
  the official contract without using oracle actions online.

Follow-up:
- Run a small Linux Habitat export with
  `objectnav_habitat_official_targetnav_local_policy_dataset` and record counts.
- Mine the post-fix FMM `tv_monitor` trace for hard local states.
- Compare unknown-as-cost FMM against learned local policy labels before
  broadening official evaluation.

Change:
- Added a core `NavigationBackend` boundary with `NavigationGoal`,
  `ExplorationRequest`, `NavigationBackendStatus`, and a
  `LegacyNavigationClientBackend` adapter for existing `send_goal` clients.
- Added `HabitatOracleFollowerBackend`, a privileged Habitat shortest-path
  follower executor for simulation upper-bound memory experiments.
- The oracle backend accepts explicit
  `NavigationGoal.metadata["habitat_goal_position"]` or a `habitat_world`
  `Pose2D` fallback, emits official discrete Habitat action names through
  `next_action()`, and marks statuses with `benchmark_valid=False` and
  `privileged_oracle=True`.
- Added fake-client and fake-Habitat unit tests for validation, go-to,
  exploration, cancel, action mapping, follower unavailability, stop handling,
  and frame guarding.
- Routed `OfficialPathfinderSuffixController` through
  `HabitatOracleFollowerBackend` so the existing diagnostic
  `memory_active_perception_frontier_pathfinder_suffix` path now exercises the
  shared backend boundary.
- Added backend-status debug payloads for the suffix controller while preserving
  the existing diagnostic-only policy name and benchmark-invalid manifest
  caveat.

Reason:
- The official memory comparison showed the current result is dominated by
  low-level FMM/TargetNav execution. A paper-quality memory study needs the
  same memory/query layer to run against swappable black-box navigators,
  including an oracle upper-bound executor that isolates memory quality from
  local-control failures.

  `0.24163039972246855`, mean distance `4.007396151311696`, one target-match
  call, and one suffix-activated episode.
  The successful episode trace records `backend_id=pathfinder_suffix_oracle`,
  `status=SUCCEEDED`, `reason=oracle_follower_stop`,
  `privileged_oracle=true`, and `benchmark_valid=false`.

Effect:
- Core memory code can now target a small navigation protocol instead of a
  specific simulation or robot client.
- Existing deterministic simulation clients can be wrapped without ROS imports.
- Habitat shortest-path execution is available as a clearly labeled oracle
  backend for upper-bound experiments, while remaining unsuitable for official
  benchmark-valid claims.
- The existing pathfinder suffix diagnostic now uses the same backend boundary,
  making the next Linux oracle smoke a real backend-boundary smoke rather than
  a separate follower shortcut.
- The backend-wired Linux smoke reproduces the earlier diagnostic success while
  making oracle privilege explicit in the recorded policy debug payload.

Follow-up:
- Add a first-class backend selector for the official memory/TargetNav policy
  path so oracle, FMM, learned, and Nav2-like executors can be compared under
  the same memory output.
- Use the backend-wired pathfinder suffix run only as failure decomposition.
- Keep official SR/SPL comparisons separate from oracle upper-bound memory
  diagnostics.

## 2026-05-31 - Execute Memory Anchors Through TargetNav Backends

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py`
- `docs/design/2026-05-31-official-memory-anchor-targetnav-backend.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-31-official-memory-anchor-targetnav-backend-smoke.md`
- `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-05-31-official-memory-anchor-targetnav-backend.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`

Change:
- Stored episode start position/rotation in `OfficialPolicyState` so an
  `episode_start_relative` memory anchor can be converted back to a Habitat
  world goal for oracle diagnostics.
- Added memory-anchor TargetNav goal payloads with source, confidence,
  category, scene, and coordinate-frame metadata.
- Added inverse `episode_start_relative x/z -> Habitat world x/y/z` conversion
  using the same yaw convention as the TargetNav dataset exporter.
- Routed matching remembered anchors through the configured TargetNav backend
  before falling back to active-perception frontier search.
- Added explicit oracle fallback debug when an anchor exists but episode start
  pose is missing.
- Added regression tests for FMM memory-anchor execution, oracle memory-anchor
  execution, and missing-start-pose fallback.

Reason:
- The previous backend selector still only activated the oracle follower after a
  fresh detector match. For the paper story, memory itself must produce the
  goal and the backend must execute it, otherwise oracle diagnostics do not
  isolate memory quality from local-control failures.

Verification:
- RED tests failed before implementation:
  `pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py::test_targetnav_fmm_backend_executes_matching_memory_anchor src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py::test_targetnav_oracle_backend_executes_matching_memory_anchor src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py::test_targetnav_oracle_memory_anchor_missing_start_pose_falls_back -q`
  produced `3 failed`.
- After implementation, the same tests produced `3 passed`.
- Local focused evaluator/CLI/navigation regression:
  `pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_objectnav_eval.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_objectnav_cli.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_navigation_backend.py -q`
  produced `117 passed`.
- Local `compileall` over the official evaluator returned cleanly.
- Local `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Synced evaluator/test/design/plan files to the Linux Habitat machine.
- Linux focused memory-anchor tests produced `3 passed`.
- Linux focused evaluator/CLI/navigation regression produced `117 passed`.
- Linux `compileall` over the official evaluator returned cleanly.
- Linux memory-anchor oracle smoke wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/targetnav_memory_anchor_oracle_backend_yolo_4ep_100steps_20260531_v1`.
  It produced success `0/4`, SPL `0.0`, SoftSPL `0.044980353551202196`,
  mean distance `5.548277020454407`, zero fresh target matches, and one
  immediate memory-anchor oracle activation in the tv-monitor episode.

Effect:
- The base TargetNav memory policy now truly executes remembered anchors through
  the selected backend.
- The oracle diagnostic path can now decompose memory-anchor quality separately
  from local-control quality.
- The first direct-memory oracle smoke is negative: the YOLO-discovered
  tv-monitor memory anchor activated the oracle backend but ended far from the
  official target, so the current prior quality is not paper-ready.

Follow-up:
- Add an oracle/ground-truth memory-prior upper-bound exporter to validate the
  transform and quantify the gap between perfect memory and discovered memory.
- Improve memory-anchor reliability with multi-view fusion, stale-memory
  detection, or learned candidate scoring before treating memory as a
  benchmark-facing win.

Change:
- Added optional `episode_id` metadata to official memory anchors.
- Updated memory-anchor selection to prefer exact episode-specific anchors
  before generic scene/category anchors.
- Added an oracle memory-prior exporter that reads privileged Habitat
  goal/viewpoint positions, converts them into `episode_start_relative` anchor
  coordinates, and writes the same memory-prior JSON schema used by the
  evaluator.
- Added CLI entry point
  `objectnav_habitat_official_oracle_memory_prior`.
- Added manifest detection for oracle memory priors:
  `source_validity=oracle_diagnostic_only` and
  `invalid_for_benchmark_claim_reason=oracle_memory_prior_diagnostic` when the
  backend itself is non-oracle.
- Added regression tests for episode ID round-trip, selector preference,
  oracle-prior manifest validity, fake-env export, coordinate conversion, and
  CLI argument pass-through.

Reason:
- The previous memory-anchor oracle smoke showed the backend/coordinate path
  works, but the YOLO-discovered memory anchor was poor. A privileged
  oracle-memory upper bound is needed to distinguish memory quality from
  downstream executor quality without changing the backend boundary.

  produced `119 passed`.

  produced `523 passed`.
- Local `compileall` over touched modules returned cleanly.

  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/oracle_memory_prior_valmini_4ep_20260531_v1/memory_prior.json`

Effect:
- The official evaluator now has a first-class oracle-memory upper-bound path
  that uses the same memory-prior file interface as discovered memory.
- Episode-specific oracle anchors avoid ambiguous selection when several
  same-scene/category episodes are exported together.
- The latest evidence decomposes the bottleneck: perfect memory helps under the
  oracle executor, while FMM remains a poor executor even with perfect memory.

Follow-up:
- Improve the oracle prior by exporting multiple candidate viewpoints or
  choosing viewpoints that better satisfy official visibility/success
  constraints.
- Use oracle memory + oracle backend as a diagnostic ceiling and
  discovered-memory + oracle backend as the memory-quality gap.
- Keep benchmark-facing claims on non-oracle memory priors and non-oracle
  backends only.

- `docs/design/2026-05-31-official-grounding-dino-memory-line.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-31-official-detector-positive-viewpoint-memory-prior.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-31-official-detector-positive-viewpoint-memory-prior.md`

Change:
- Added optional per-viewpoint trace JSON and annotated RGB image artifacts to
  the official detector-positive viewpoint prior exporter.
- Added CLI flags `--viewpoint-trace-output` and `--viewpoint-image-dir`.
- Added ObjectNav category aliasing to Grounding-DINO prompts and label
  mapping, currently including `tv_monitor -> tv monitor. television. tv.`.
- Added RED/GREEN tests for trace rows, debug image output, CLI forwarding, and
  canonical mapping of `tv monitor` detections back to `tv_monitor`.

Reason:
- The first detector-positive viewpoint diagnostic reached `3/4`; the remaining
  `tv_monitor` miss needed evidence. The trace showed DINO restored all
  viewpoints but never emitted the canonical target label. Raw category prompts
  with underscores are not good language prompts.

Verification:
- RED detector-viewpoint tests failed before implementation with missing
  `viewpoint_trace_path`, missing `viewpoint_image_dir`, and missing CLI flags.
- RED Grounding-DINO alias test failed because the prompt was `tv_monitor.`
  instead of `tv monitor. television. tv.`.
- Local Grounding-DINO adapter tests produced `6 passed`.
- Local focused regression:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_detector_viewpoint_memory_prior.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_oracle_memory_prior.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_memory_discovery_cli.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_objectnav_cli.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_grounding_dino_adapter.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_ros_packaging.py -q`
  produced `35 passed`.
- Local `compileall` over the new exporter/CLI and Grounding-DINO adapter
  returned cleanly.
- Local `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Remote focused regression in conda env `habitat` produced `35 passed`.
- Remote `compileall` over the same modules returned cleanly.
- Remote pre-alias viewpoint trace confirmed the skipped `tv_monitor` episode
  had `32` restored viewpoints, `18` detections, `0` target matches, and labels
  `plant`/`bed`.
- Remote alias viewpoint trace wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/grounding_dino_detector_positive_viewpoint_trace_alias_4ep_32vp_20260531_v1`;
  it exported `4` anchors, restored `6` viewpoints, had `12` detections, and
  skipped `0` episodes.
- Remote alias viewpoint prior + oracle TargetNav backend wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/grounding_dino_detector_positive_viewpoint_alias_oracle_backend_4ep_100steps_20260531_v1`;
  it produced SR `4/4`, SPL `0.8134277193790571`, SoftSPL
  `0.8060506098824843`, and mean distance-to-goal `0.04706096462905407`.
- Remote opportunistic alias DINO discovery wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/grounding_dino_discovery_prior_alias_4ep_100steps_20260531_v1`;
  it exported `17` anchors from `666` detections.
- Remote opportunistic alias prior + oracle TargetNav backend wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/grounding_dino_discovery_prior_alias_oracle_backend_4ep_100steps_20260531_v1`;
  it still produced SR `0/4`, SPL `0.0`, SoftSPL `0.25094861127490686`, and
  mean distance-to-goal `5.419067084789276`.

Effect:
- The detector-positive viewpoint diagnostic now reaches a clean `4/4` on the
  first four official episodes under oracle-backend evaluation.
- Category aliasing fixes a real DINO/ObjectNav interface bug and improves
  opportunistic discovery coverage from `1` to `17` anchors.
- The online-ish opportunistic projected anchors remain poor despite the alias
  fix, so the next real method gap is learned/non-privileged viewpoint-anchor
  generation rather than more detector prompt polishing.

Follow-up:
- Build a non-privileged candidate-viewpoint proposer and memory-anchor
  generator that approximates detector-positive viewpoints from exploration
  observations.
- Keep the privileged viewpoint exporter as a diagnostic ceiling only.
- Validate category aliases on larger splits before claiming general detector
  improvements.

## 2026-05-31 - Anchor Quality Diagnostic and Robot Viewpoint Memory Mode

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_official_memory_anchor_quality.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/report_habitat_official_memory_anchor_quality.py`

- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/run_habitat_official_memory_discovery.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_memory_anchor_quality.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_official_episode_memory.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_memory_discovery.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_memory_discovery_cli.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_ros_packaging.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/setup.py`
- `docs/design/2026-05-31-official-memory-anchor-quality-diagnostic.md`
- `docs/design/2026-05-31-official-robot-viewpoint-memory-anchor.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-31-official-memory-anchor-quality-diagnostic.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-31-official-robot-viewpoint-memory-anchor.md`

Change:
- Added an offline official memory-anchor quality report with JSON/CSV/Markdown
  artifacts and CLI entry point
  `objectnav_habitat_official_memory_anchor_quality`.
- The report compares candidate anchors against a reference prior using the
  same episode wildcard semantics as the evaluator, while separately reporting
  exact episode matches and wildcard matches.
- Fixed official memory discovery to write structured `episode_id` on exported
  anchors, not only in the source string.
- Added `--anchor-mode robot_viewpoint` to official memory discovery. This mode
  stores the detector-positive robot pose instead of a bbox-depth projected
  object-center estimate.

Reason:
- The detector-positive viewpoint diagnostic now reaches `4/4`, while
  opportunistic projected anchors remain `0/4`. We needed a direct report of
  localization, ranking, and coverage errors.
- The user proposed the core representation pivot: remember where the robot was
  when it saw the object, then use camera/SLAM/local control to reacquire and
  approach nearby.

Verification:
- RED anchor-quality tests failed on missing module/CLI/setup entry, then
  passed after implementation.
- RED wildcard matching test exposed the discovery prior's missing
  `episode_id` fields; discovery export now writes exact episode ids.
- RED robot-viewpoint discovery/CLI tests failed on missing `anchor_mode`, then
  passed after implementation.
- Local focused regression:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_official_episode_memory.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_memory_discovery.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_memory_discovery_cli.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_memory_anchor_quality.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_detector_viewpoint_memory_prior.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_grounding_dino_adapter.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_ros_packaging.py -q`
  produced `36 passed`.
- Local `compileall` over touched modules returned cleanly.
- Local `git diff --check` returned cleanly.
- Remote focused regression in conda env `habitat` produced `25 passed`.
- Remote fixed projected DINO discovery wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/grounding_dino_discovery_prior_alias_episode_ids_4ep_100steps_20260531_v1`
  with `17` anchors and exact episode ids.
- Remote projected-anchor quality vs detector-positive viewpoints:
  nearest mean error `5.050678 m`, selected mean error `5.197824 m`,
  nearest-good count `0/4`.
- Remote projected-anchor quality vs oracle object anchors:
  nearest mean error `5.657046 m`, selected mean error `5.798235 m`,
  nearest-good count `1/4`.
- Remote robot-viewpoint DINO discovery wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/grounding_dino_robot_viewpoint_prior_4ep_100steps_20260531_v1`
  with `17` anchors and `anchor_mode=robot_viewpoint`.
- Remote robot-viewpoint quality vs detector-positive viewpoints:
  nearest mean error `6.378549 m`, selected mean error `6.742113 m`,
  nearest-good count `0/4`.
- Remote robot-viewpoint quality vs oracle object anchors:
  nearest mean error `5.049185 m`, selected mean error `5.398587 m`,
  nearest-good count `0/4`.
- Remote robot-viewpoint prior + oracle TargetNav backend wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/grounding_dino_robot_viewpoint_oracle_backend_4ep_100steps_20260531_v1`;
  it produced SR `0/4`, SPL `0.0`, SoftSPL `0.0`, and mean distance-to-goal
  `5.950337052345276`.

Effect:
- We now have a reusable anchor-quality diagnostic that quantifies why a memory
  prior fails before spending time on navigation backend changes.
- Episode ids are now first-class in newly exported discovery priors, avoiding
  accidental cross-episode wildcard memory use.
- Naively storing the first detector-positive robot pose is not enough. It is
  structurally closer to the desired representation, but the memory should be
  committed after detector-guided approach or visual confirmation.

Follow-up:
- Implement a detector-confirmed approach/servo stage before committing
  `robot_viewpoint` anchors.
- Preserve detection bbox/depth/bearing evidence for local reacquisition.
- Add Habitat-backed visibility/navigability/floor checks to the quality
  report.

Change:
- Added first-class official ObjectNav policy aliases for
  `no_memory_targetnav` and `naive_count_targetnav`.
- Updated the official memory comparison default mapping so
  `memory_guided`, `no_memory`, and `naive_count` share the same terminal
  TargetNav backend.
- Forwarded a shared `--targetnav-backend` through the comparison CLI/runner.
- Recorded the completed 4-episode Grounding-DINO targetnav-equated smoke.

Reason:
- The paper-facing comparison should test memory target quality, not whether
  different rows use different terminal navigation controllers.
- On the real robot, terminal goal execution will be a Nav2-like black-box
  substrate, so the simulation scaffold should isolate the memory layer.

Verification:
- Local focused tests for official ObjectNav eval, memory comparison, CLI, and
  packaging produced `118 passed`.
- Remote focused tests in the Linux `habitat` conda env produced `118 passed`.
- Local and remote `compileall` checks over the touched evaluator,
  comparison, and CLI modules were clean.
- Remote comparison artifact
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/targetnav_equated_grounding_dino_memory_comparison_4ep_100steps_20260531_v1/comparison.json`
  was inspected and reports Habitat `env.get_metrics` as the metric source.
- Local `git diff --check` was clean after the documentation update.
- A trailing-whitespace scan over the updated design, experiment, handoff, and
  devlog files found no matches.

Effect:
- The comparison scaffold is now a cleaner memory-quality diagnostic:
  `memory_guided` uses `memory_active_perception_frontier_targetnav`,
  `no_memory` uses `no_memory_targetnav`, and `naive_count` uses
  `naive_count_targetnav`.
- The first targetnav-equated smoke is negative for current memory writes:
  `no_memory` reached SR `0.5000` and SPL `0.4673`, while both memory rows
  remained at SR `0.0000` and SPL `0.0000`.

Follow-up:
- Keep this targetnav-equated scaffold as the main comparison harness.
- Improve memory write/selection policy around confirmed viewpoints before
  scaling the table or tuning terminal navigation.

Change:
- Added `anchor_selection_policy=confidence|view_quality` to official memory
  discovery.
- Preserved the old confidence-based ranking as the default.
- Added CLI forwarding for `--anchor-selection-policy`.
- Added detection CSV fields for bbox area, center offset, depth evidence, and
  selection policy.
- Added tests proving `view_quality` can prefer a lower-confidence but larger,
  centered robot-viewpoint candidate while the default stays confidence-ranked.

Reason:
- The targetnav-equated comparison showed memory target quality is the current
  bottleneck.
- Confidence is a detector belief, not a navigation-memory quality measure; a
  target-visible viewpoint should prefer large, centered evidence before
  later learned write policies are trained.

Verification:
- RED tests failed before implementation on unsupported
  `anchor_selection_policy`.
- Local focused tests passed:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_memory_discovery.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_memory_discovery_cli.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_ros_packaging.py -q`
  produced `17 passed`.
- Local `compileall` over the touched discovery module, CLI, and tests was
  clean.
- Synced touched code/tests/design to `badger@100.88.131.52`.
- Remote focused tests in conda env `habitat` produced `17 passed`; remote
  `compileall` for the touched discovery module and CLI was clean.
- Remote Grounding-DINO view-quality discovery wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/grounding_dino_robot_viewpoint_view_quality_prior_4ep_100steps_20260531_v1`.
- Remote anchor quality and oracle-backend query artifacts completed.

Effect:
- The first view-quality smoke is a negative diagnostic:
  `3` anchors exported, selected mean error `6.891912 m` vs detector-positive
  viewpoint references, selected mean error `5.543938 m` vs oracle anchors,
  and oracle-backend query SR `0/4`, SPL `0.0`, SoftSPL `0.0`.
- This narrows the failure: passive detection ranking cannot solve memory
  writes when exploration does not reach useful confirmed viewpoints.

Follow-up:
- Keep `view_quality` as a diagnostic baseline.
- Move next to online write/option utility labels from actual detector approach
  or scan rollouts before committing memory.

## 2026-05-31 - Confirmed Detector-Approach Memory Write Diagnostic

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/evaluation/habitat_official_memory_discovery.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/objectnav_core/cli/run_habitat_official_memory_discovery.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_memory_discovery.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_memory_discovery_cli.py`
- `docs/design/2026-05-31-official-confirmed-detector-approach-memory-write.md`
- `docs/experiments/2026-05-31-official-confirmed-detector-approach-memory-write.md`
- `docs/handoff/2026-05-29-closed-loop-dual-anchor-habitat-objectnav.md`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`

Change:
- Added `anchor_commit_policy=confirmed_detector_approach` to official memory
  discovery.
- The new policy uses detector-guided local control after target-category
  detections, writes only when the controller would range-confirm the target,
  and drops unconfirmed weak glimpses instead of storing bad memory.
- Added summary counts for confirmed and unconfirmed detector-approach attempts.
- Added RED/GREEN tests for confirmed write and unconfirmed-drop behavior, plus
  CLI forwarding.
- Ran strict confirmed and non-strict long-budget Grounding-DINO diagnostics.

Reason:
- The user correctly pointed out that exploration should approach a detected
  target before writing memory. The previous passive write/ranking policies
  could keep frontier exploration and store weak first-glimpse viewpoints.
- A paper-quality lifelong memory system should write from confirmed useful
  viewpoints, not from accidental detections.

Verification:
- RED tests failed before implementation on unsupported
  `confirmed_detector_approach`.
- Local focused tests:
  `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTHONPATH=src/objectnav_core python3 -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_memory_discovery.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_memory_discovery_cli.py src/objectnav_core/tests/test_ros_packaging.py -q`
  produced `20 passed`.
- Local `compileall` over touched discovery modules and tests was clean.
- Remote focused tests in conda env `habitat` produced `20 passed`.
- Remote `compileall` over touched discovery modules and tests was clean.
- Local `git diff --check` was clean.
- Touched-file trailing-whitespace scan found no matches.
- Remote strict confirmed Grounding-DINO discovery wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/grounding_dino_robot_viewpoint_confirmed_detector_approach_prior_4ep_100steps_20260531_v1`
  with `633` detections, `46` detector-approach deferred actions, `60`
  unconfirmed attempts, and `0` exported anchors.
- Remote non-strict `detector_approach` budget-8 discovery wrote
  `runs/habitat_official_objectnav/grounding_dino_robot_viewpoint_detector_approach8_prior_4ep_100steps_20260531_v1`
  with `2` anchors.
- Non-strict anchor quality stayed poor: selected mean error `6.262038 m` vs
  detector-positive viewpoint references, `5.752019 m` vs oracle anchors, and
  `0` good anchors.
- Non-strict query with `memory_active_perception_frontier_targetnav` and
  `targetnav_backend=oracle_follower` stayed at SR `0/4`, SPL `0.0`, SoftSPL
  `0.003394134213343364`.

Effect:
- The discovery loop is now explicitly target-reactive: it can stop writing bad
  memories when the detector approach never reaches a confirmed view.
- The negative smoke narrows the next bottleneck to target tracking/local
  detector servo quality, not memory ranking or terminal TargetNav.
- This also clarifies real-robot transfer: SLAM/Nav2 can help local approach
  and return-to-memory execution, but the memory representation should still be
  confirmed visual/geometric evidence rather than a brittle long-lived map
  coordinate.

Follow-up:
- Add an approach-attempt trace artifact with per-step pose, bbox, depth, and
  chosen detector action.
- Build a stronger target-tracking approach option that keeps visual lock,
  scans when the detector is lost, and commits only after view quality improves.
- Keep targetnav-equated evaluation fixed while improving the memory write
  option.

## 2026-05-31 - Official Habitat Scaffold Commit Hygiene

File:
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_candidate_rollout_dataset.py`
- `src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_candidate_rollout_hard_state_mining.py`
- `docs/devlog/2026-05.md`

Change:
- Aligned two CLI `python -m ... --help` subprocess tests with the existing
  local package-resolution convention by running the child process from
  `src/objectnav_core`.

Reason:
- The parent pytest process receives `pythonpath = ["src/objectnav_core"]` from
  `pyproject.toml`, but subprocesses do not inherit pytest path injection as an
  import path. The matching TargetNav local-policy CLI guard test already used
  `cwd=Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]`.

Verification:
- Initial full local suite reproduced the issue: `550 passed`, `2 failed`, both
  failures were `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'objectnav_core'` in
  subprocess CLI guard tests.
- Focused rerun after the path fix:
  `python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_candidate_rollout_dataset.py::test_candidate_rollout_dataset_cli_module_has_main_guard src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_candidate_rollout_hard_state_mining.py::test_hard_state_miner_cli_module_has_main_guard src/objectnav_core/tests/test_habitat_official_targetnav_local_policy_dataset.py::test_targetnav_local_policy_dataset_cli_module_has_main_guard`
  produced `3 passed`.
- Full local rerun:
  `python -m pytest src/objectnav_core/tests`
  produced `552 passed`.

Effect:
- The official Habitat scaffold test suite is locally runnable from the repo
  root without relying on an editable install in the active interpreter.
